# FloodPulse development log

Append a dated entry after each future Codex task. Preserve earlier entries and distinguish verified results, pending work and failures. Use actual repository files, manifests, tests and Git history; record a delivery's full hash in the next entry after confirming it, without making an extra commit solely to record its own hash.

## Verified project status at log creation — 4 October 2026

The existing React/Vite → FastAPI → Open-Meteo weather dashboard, Udupi historical flood map and optional **Drainage Research Layers** view remain in place. This task verified their isolated contracts and browser rendering; it made no live weather request. The protected local Udupi history has 63 rainfall records and two event-evidence records. Historical map events **2728 (14–30 September 2005)** and **3551 (25 September–12 October 2009)** retain **33/23 observation-qualified non-permanent flood cells**, representing event-window observations rather than daily or current flood claims. The drainage study is a documented 3 km × 3 km window with real surface elevation, slope and 2021 land cover; no drain/ditch ways were found in its bounded OSM query, leaving unmapped infrastructure unknown.

The local Survey of India 2025 edition supplies 31 valid Karnataka district polygons, including Vijayanagara. Its actual CRS and same-release state coverage were validated; raw archives and geometry remain local. Current-LGD reconciliation is incomplete: zero independently verified current-LGD identities, 12 exact NIC name candidates and 19 unresolved matches. SOI-supplied codes are source identifiers only. CHIRPS monthly partitions now cover **January, February, March and August 2025**, with **3751 complete district-day observations**, native 0.05° grid, original masks and local cell-centre aggregation. Rainfall publication rights for SOI-derived aggregates remain unresolved. There is no completed annual product or historical backfill.

Shelter navigation remains unpublished: Thekkatte and Padu-Kapu MPCS government designations were identified, but reliable facility entrance coordinates and current operational status remain unverified. No flood-prediction model, validated drainage-risk score or Telegram warning integration is claimed. See [the maintained source register](docs/DATA_SOURCE_REGISTER.md) for source identities, terms, provenance and limitations.

Verified Git history includes the application/data checkpoint `3eb084cfd95ce2e2895cc70b5a5dd7921745eea5`, SOI verification `c325728accdc45445f2ca63227f6166aaf017db8`, August completion `a21f43439b73e7be200f36740bcd692c90d380f8`, and January–February/March interruption checkpoint `7a7dd8689b9ec644d4f7fe080e18d857f6a07e8c`. The last hash was independently confirmed on GitHub at the start of this task. Earlier task details have not been reconstructed beyond these verified artifacts.

## 4 October 2026 — Stage 2B Recovery: Complete March 2025

**Objective and completed work:** investigate the 5 March metadata timeout, add bounded request recovery, reuse validated 1–4 March downloads and complete March without changing geographic or rainfall methodology. The original metadata query was necessary, not duplicated. Its former 10-second network timeout did not establish an absent image. Recovery used a configurable **60-second initialized EE transport deadline**, at most **three attempts** with **2/4-second** backoffs and **zero SDK automatic retries**, within the existing **900-second monthly wall budget**. The initialization itself remains bounded by that total budget. Permanent source/authorization/validation errors are not retried; ambiguous source-not-found messages are not equated to verified absence. Downloads keep existing byte/time limits and preserve any failed partial attempt. Failure provenance now records the failed operation, category, attempt count and completed/unreviewed dates.

**Actual source coverage:** project `floodpulse` successfully retrieved CHIRPS v2 assets `UCSB-CHG/CHIRPS/DAILY/20250305` through `/20250331` sequentially. All 27 metadata, 27 download-URL and 27 download operations succeeded on their first attempts; no live retries were needed. Original 1–4 March rasters were copied byte-for-byte without new source queries. New retrievals occurred **2026-10-04T16:39:30.302346+00:00–16:40:53.144384+00:00**; reused observations retain their original retrieval times. All **961 March records** have complete valid native-cell coverage, with zero missing/partial observations. The four completed months total **3751** records. Source units remain **mm/day**, the grid remains **0.05° EPSG:4326**, and all 2025 SOI identifiers remain pending independent current-LGD verification. No SOI polygon was transmitted externally.

**Created datasets:**

- Local immutable `data/working/karnataka_chirps_soi2025_202503_v1/`: daily table and full manifest.
- Local `data/raw/chirps/karnataka_window_202503_recovered_v1/`: 31 original-resolution rasters, retrieval log and request-attempt log; **690622 bytes** including the working table/manifest.
- Permitted `data/reference/karnataka_chirps_202503_v1/manifest.json`: **71948 bytes** of source/query/coverage/provenance/checksum metadata, without rainfall statistics or restricted geometry.

**Modified code/documents:** `scripts/extract_karnataka_rainfall.py`, `scripts/assemble_karnataka_annual.py`, `backend/tests/test_karnataka_rainfall.py`, `backend/tests/test_karnataka_annual.py` and `docs/DATA_SOURCE_REGISTER.md`; created `backend/tests/test_karnataka_rainfall_recovery.py` and this `update.md`. The annual loader change only reads the actual raw path declared by the recovered month's manifest, rejects paths outside local CHIRPS storage and preserves the legacy fallback. No annual assembly ran. Original interrupted-March artifacts, all previously completed versions, application files, stylesheet and untracked `QandA.md` remain unchanged.

**Verification:** **251 backend tests passed** with blocked real sockets and disposable fixtures; **14 frontend unit tests**, **four Chromium browser regressions** and the **production build** passed. Existing validators passed for Udupi historical/flood-spatial/drainage datasets, provisional NIC inventory, SOI geometry, January, February, August and the one-day August smoke. March passed full offline raster-to-table reproduction and nine independent scalar comparisons for source codes 565, 761 and 569 on 1, 15 and 31 March. All **203 starting protected files** retained exact SHA-256, size and modification time; copied March 1–4 bytes also match their originals.

**Failures and remaining limitations:** the previous March timeout/failure log is preserved as evidence; this recovery had no failed live operations. Initial test collection found an unnecessary optional HTTP-library import in the offline backend environment; lazy exception classification fixed it without changing dependencies, and the final suite passed. A pre-existing FastAPI/Starlette test-client deprecation warning remains. SOI-derived rainfall redistribution permission, current-LGD reconciliation and shelter entrance verification remain unresolved. Historical estimates are not gauges or proven prediction-time-available inputs. No April extraction, annual dataset, training, negative labels or drainage-risk scoring was performed.

**Git delivery:** branch `feature/karnataka-data-foundation`; previous verified checkpoint **`7a7dd8689b9ec644d4f7fe080e18d857f6a07e8c`**; configured origin `git@github.com:Codovia/FloodThings.git`. Recovery code, tests, source register, this log and permitted March provenance are prepared for a reviewed commit/push. Raw rasters, archives, restricted geometries, rainfall tables and `QandA.md` are excluded. The final task response records the new full commit hash and independently verified push result; the next task should append that confirmed hash here.

**Next proposed task:** separately authorize sequential April–June 2025 collection, preserving these four immutable monthly versions and the existing method. An annual dataset remains conditional on all twelve validated months; independent current-LGD reconciliation and publication permission require separate resolution.

Bijapur valid 166123/166424(99.82%),301 unusable,765 permanent-water cells; Raichur 132615/135207(98.08%),2592 unusable,854 permanent-water cells. GFD remains 250 m/EPSG:32643/nearest masks and data. Original IFI 2698 single-day 2005-08-01 and 3107 single-day 2007-06-27 remain provisional and separate. No satellite query/reduction; all original counts/statuses/manifests preserved.

**Methodology rationale:** IFI positive-report inventory is incomplete; absent reports and masked/unavailable satellite observations cannot certify flood absence. Random backgrounds may include unrecorded floods; distant/dry-season sampling may produce easy geography/season distinctions and misleading performance. Evidence vocabulary explicitly includes satellite_positive, observed_zero_event, unlabeled_background, insufficient_observation, candidate_mismatch. Four positives and two observed-zero comparisons enter the pilot; seven insufficient records stay excluded. Future screened backgrounds must match geography/season/period/availability/coverage and be checked against known IFI/GFD/verified evidence; they remain unlabeled even after screening. No background records created.

**Leakage/non-binary safeguards:** exact 30 pre-start dates; shared positive/comparison feature engineering; strict nested antecedent/descriptive schemas; approved predictor accessor excludes descriptive fields; all 6 `ml_binary_label` values null; binary target accessor raises a review-required error. Tests reject event-start/future/window leakage, IFI-expanded windows, unknown/binary/risk fields, insufficient/unlabeled examples and weakened protected counts. No random date generation. Validators reconstruct every daily and derived value from retained source rasters; all 98 independent scalar checks pass.

**Descriptive findings/readiness:** for each 1/3/7/14/30-day antecedent sum, both observed-zero values lie within the minimum–maximum range of the four positives. This marginal range overlap is descriptive, not an accuracy/threshold claim. Rainfall histories differ across six selectively reviewed scopes. Bijapur/Raichur differ in geography, season and year from positives and are not matched controls or true absence observations. No accuracy, AUC, causal claim or rainfall threshold. Recommend **continued evidence collection**, not training. Supervised learning needs defensible negatives; PU needs reviewed representative unlabeled windows, more positives, selection-bias/prevalence assumptions and independent evaluation; ranking needs a defined comparable event population/outcome and validation. Classic PU random-positive-selection assumptions are unverified for this inventory/satellite selection. No final ML approach selected. Historical CHIRPS Final release timing and GFD-vs-IFI onset differences remain limitations.

**Actual homogeneous groups:** Sentinel-1A, IW, descending, relative orbit 63, actual dual VV/VH, 10 m grid class. Udupi baseline 2018-04-16/04-28/05-10/05-22; event 2018-06-03, **+5 days after** IFI. Kodagu baseline 2018-11-30/12-24; event 2019-01-05, **+1 day after**. Per-pixel median baseline in dB, event-minus-baseline in dB; no after-event baseline or dB division. Used/rejected original scene IDs/timestamps/configuration/footprints and native VV/VH/angle projections retained. Approximate incidence-band nominal scale is ~16 km, not independent 10 m incidence measurements. Common 10 m nearest UTM alignment is explicit; it is not preservation of native acquisition-grid cell identity or guaranteed physical spatial resolution.

**Scope/workload/method:** deterministic 2 km square at each public district representative point, not an independently located flood site, municipal boundary or full district/event footprint. Local public-polygon cell-centre clip, no SOI transmitted. Two 40,000-cell windows; four half-open 100x100 tiles each; 19/13 bands, 190,000/130,000 band-cells per tile, retained 300,000 ceiling, no remote reductions/exports. Original masks and masked sentinel values retained. Raw VV/VH/angle stacks and georeferenced EPSG:32643 baseline/event/change products remain local. Original SAR observations were not overwritten.

**Predeclared sensitivity/limitations:** both polarizations must decrease -2/-3/-4 dB, event VV -14/-16/-18 dB and VH -22 dB; slope 3/5/7 percent via 100*tan(SRTM slope degrees); incidence 30–45 degrees and <=1-degree before/event difference. Nominal -3/-16/-22 dB and 5% is experimental, not validated. All 27 change/VV/slope settings retained; no best-fit/IFI tuning, smoothing or connected-pixel filter. UN-SPIDER supports acquisition/water/terrain controls but does not validate these numeric settings. GSW v1.4 seasonality >=10 months excludes permanent water only where original seasonality/occurrence masks are valid; masked cells remain unknown. GSW 1984–2021 context is retrospective, never a prediction-time feature. SRTM circa 2000/~30 m surface slope is not direct shadow/layover correction. Speckle, vegetation, double bounce and smooth non-water surfaces remain unresolved.

**Actual results/corroboration:** both stacks have **40,000/40,000 valid SAR cells**, full retained DEM coverage, but **40,000 missing JRC auxiliary cells**, leaving **zero usable analysis cells** each. Stored nominal arithmetic candidate count/area is 0/0, marked **not_assessable_no_valid_cells**; this does not certify no water/no flood. All 27 sensitivity counts are zero over empty usable masks, so stability is **not assessable**. Event incidence ranges Udupi 42.69–42.82 degrees/Kodagu 36.26–36.41 degrees. Raw VV changes span approximately -9.75 to +9.39 dB/-11.56 to +8.62 dB, VH -10.92 to +8.26/-18.46 to +12.16 dB; these changes are not verified water. **No independent spatial corroboration, no supported-candidate promotion.** Mandya's retained provisional GFD4508 association remains distinct; earlier GFD review was Gadag/insufficient, with no comparable Mandya SAR pair. No GFD fusion/extent-equality claim.

Each has **40,000 unmasked angle cells, 40,000 valid VV/VH cells and 40,000 intersections**. These are cells on the retained 10 m processing grid, not independent 10 m angle measurements. Source approximate angle nominal scale is about 16.083 km; the catalogue lists 20 km. Full source IDs/projections, masks and footprints remain local.

Angle statistics in degrees (linear quantiles; full precision in versioned metadata):

| Scene date | Minimum | Maximum | Mean | Median | P1 | P5 | P25 | P75 | P95 | P99 |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 2021-07-05 | 45.547394 | 45.687576 | 45.616174 | 45.615742 | 45.553829 | 45.562267 | 45.586074 | 45.646035 | 45.671299 | 45.680492 |
| 2021-07-17 | 45.547298 | 45.687149 | 45.615871 | 45.615425 | 45.553707 | 45.562115 | 45.585837 | 45.645660 | 45.670887 | 45.680069 |
| 2021-07-29 | 45.555233 | 45.696041 | 45.624474 | 45.624092 | 45.561733 | 45.570259 | 45.594257 | 45.654477 | 45.679768 | 45.688961 |

Baseline is the per-pixel median of the two original baseline angles. Absolute baseline/event difference: **minimum 0.007884979°, maximum 0.008682251°, mean 0.008451454°, median 0.008506775°**; P1=0.007968903°, P5=0.008068085°, P25=0.008304596°, P75=0.008628845°, P95=0.008674622°, P99=0.008678436°. Independent scalar interpolation/medians/float32 differences reproduce every usable cell. **40,000/40,000 satisfy the preserved ≤1° agreement diagnostic; 0 satisfy the legacy 30–45° absolute range.** No gate, threshold or stored Stage 3E4 result changed.

[Earth Engine's catalogue](https://developers.google.com/earth-engine/datasets/catalog/COPERNICUS_S1_GRD) defines approximate incidence **from the ellipsoid**, not from the terrain normal. Local terrain incidence is a different quantity and was not computed. [Copernicus SentiWiki IW characteristics](https://sentiwiki.copernicus.eu/web/s1-mission) give 29.1–46.0°. The [Google-hosted SAR tutorial](https://developers.google.com/earth-engine/tutorials/community/sar-basics) describes 30–39° for its particular AOI; it is not a universal validity rule. Git history first introduces 30–45° in **ecacf535a63cc32df100250de74491861885498f**, in code and a method explicitly describing numerical gates as experimental and not validated by UN-SPIDER. Retained original Git objects/excerpts/checksums support **project_heuristic / legacy_angle_filter_unsubstantiated**. No sufficiently authoritative basis for exactly 30–45° was found. The archived rule remains solely for reproduction, not as established scientific validity.

Far range is an inference from the approximately 45.6° ellipsoid angles near the documented IW upper range. Subswath membership is not independently verified. Projected minimum tile-to-scene-boundary distances are **2248.139 m / 2272.570 m / 2116.918 m**, respectively; this is actual footprint geometry, not an arbitrary edge-mask threshold. No automatic far-range rejection or diagnosed temporal geometry inconsistency.

Terrain uses the existing project source **USGS/SRTMGL1_003**, with one bounded four-band subset of this exact window because the existing Udupi raster does not cover Belagavi. Elevation **522–542 m**, mean **531.9978 m**. Slope degrees: minimum **0**, maximum **20.571659**, mean **2.148843**, median **1.854334**, P25 **0.968035**, P75 **2.679470**, P95 **4.757452**, P99 **15.101592**. All 40,000 elevation/slope cells valid. Native approximately 30.922 m EPSG:4326 DSM; native [four-connected ee.Terrain.slope](https://developers.google.com/earth-engine/apidocs/ee-terrain-slope), nearest sampled to the retained 10 m UTM43N grid. February 2000 surface elevations are not contemporaneous 2021 bare earth. Predominantly gentle sampled terrain contains steeper local cells; slope alone cannot certify absent layover/shadow, bank/building effects or local incidence. No steepness threshold/fraction, local-incidence formula or terrain mask invented.

The [official public NRSC GeoServer landing page](https://bhuvan-gp1.nrsc.gov.in/bhuvan/web/) advertises WCS/WFS/WMS/WMTS. Only advertised capabilities URLs were used, at most two attempts per service and no private namespace/credential probing. **WFS:** HTTP 200 with an OWS exception explicitly saying `Service WFS is disabled`; not valid capabilities. **WCS:** two bounded attempts, ReadTimeout then streaming ConnectionError; HTTP 200 headers on the second response but incomplete XML, not successful retrieval. **WMS:** two bounded streaming ConnectionErrors after HTTP 200 headers, incomplete XML. **WMTS:** one HTTP 400, GWC reports it cannot locate a configured layer/group and its configuration is out of sync. Original responses/checksums/retrieval times are retained, including first-pass discovery and the subsequent single advertised WMTS check; no source pages or failed services re-requested during that completion. No further live discovery is scheduled.

[Public flood inventory](https://bhuvan-app1.nrsc.gov.in/disaster/usrtasks/flood/flood.php?uname=empty) confirms five July Karnataka products: **ka_2021_25_07**, **ka_2021_25-27_07**, **ka_2021_28_07**, **ka_2021_29_07**, **ka_2021_29_07_18**, with literal dates 25, 25–27, 28, 29 and 29 July 18Hr. No separate July cumulative product verified beyond the advertised multi-date entry; aggregated 2003–2020 maps do not establish 2021 coverage. These are catalogue-only; portal zoom extents are not product footprints and actual overlap with this tile is **unverified**. No WFS features, WCS coverage, WMS image, rendered-color polygon or manually digitized PDF retrieved as inundation truth.

**Selected feature/code:** `GET /api/weather` now accepts paired finite latitude (−90..90) and longitude (−180..180). No parameters retain the original adapter path. Invalid/incomplete pairs return HTTP 422 without a provider call. New `backend/app/location_weather.py` copies existing request parameters, reuses the validated parser, makes one provider call with a 10-second absolute deadline, preserves nulls and reports explicit unavailable responses. Provider-grid positions and requested coordinates differ legitimately and are never relabelled as gauge/locality measurements. No archives, database writes, synthetic fallback or retry loop.

New `WeatherLocationSelector` provides manual WGS84 input, expandable Leaflet map, Karnataka camera/reset controls and GPS permission only after a button action. It shows device-reported accuracy where available, a tile-unavailable notice and coordinate/privacy explanation. App clears obsolete readings on selection/failure, rejects mismatched response points, ignores late responses and supports repeated reset/refresh. New CSS keeps existing stylesheet bytes unchanged. Marker is only a requested location; no flood shapes are approximated or risk zones created. Existing historical/drainage components are unchanged.

**Real-data demonstration:** with existing README backend/frontend startup, enter latitude `13.3419169`, longitude `74.7473232`, choose **Get point weather**, then optionally open the weather map and choose another point. GPS is optional and denied permission leaves manual controls usable. Show requested versus provider coordinates, current valid time, retrieval time, three forecast validity dates, attribution and prediction-unavailable state. The reference is the existing verified Udupi OSM node 245620117; it does not establish municipal containment.

One bounded real browser → Vite → FastAPI → Open-Meteo request succeeded (HTTP 200, available, three days). Requested point 13.3419169/74.7473232; returned grid 13.321617/74.79452. Real values rendered in Chromium, screenshot visually inspected, no JavaScript errors. Twenty allowed tile requests were a deliberate external-request budget; fallback exercised without claiming provider tile outage. Local health, historical catalogue, event 2728 (33 features), event 3551 (23 features), drainage metadata all returned HTTP 200. All live weather values were available; missing-data/error behavior verified with isolated controlled fixtures, never written into scientific datasets.

## 2026-10-08 — Application MVP Sprint 2: seven-day weather complete

**Objective/status:** completed the existing React → FastAPI → Open-Meteo seven-day weather feature. This is environmental information, not a validated seven-day flood prediction. Stage 5B remains deferred; ML training, feature matrices, risk thresholds, alerts and synthetic observations remain prohibited. Current prediction-unavailable notice and selected-point API status preserved.

**Starting inspection:** `feature/karnataka-data-foundation`, HEAD/previous verified commit `b987a4b59c58f77d90ce27434eb5ff6cf23def95`, origin `git@github.com:Codovia/FloodThings.git`. Inspected current weather/parser/request/API, React selectors/dashboard, tests, README/specification context, source register, prior progress and immutable manifests. Master/Codovia specification files remain absent; preserved QandA was read as historical intent only. Initial working tree contained independent update.md whitespace and untracked QandA.md, both preserved outside the commit. No destructive Git operations or credential access.

**Backend/API:** existing `backend/app/weather.py` now requests `forecast_days=7` and daily `precipitation_sum,temperature_2m_max,temperature_2m_min,weather_code`, with Celsius/mm and Asia/Kolkata. Existing `/api/weather` endpoint, default Bengaluru and paired WGS84 point queries are preserved; original units/time/current data, source attribution, requested-versus-grid identity and invalid-coordinate 422 contract remain. Both adapters have a 10-second absolute provider-call deadline, one request, no retry/substitute/persistence. Provider errors and invalid payloads return 503 without readings.

Forecast dates are validated as canonical ISO dates, unique, ascending and inside the requested local-calendar week. The parser accepts 0–7 actual rows, including gaps; it never pads/synthesizes days. Optional weather-code omission and null values remain unavailable. Wrong units, mismatched arrays, invalid weather codes/types, inverted temperature ranges, out-of-window/duplicate/reordered dates and missing provider grid metadata are rejected. New per-row `weather_code`; additive `forecast_units` and `forecast_coverage` (requested/returned/valid counts, missing dates, complete date coverage, start/end, timezone, local-calendar-day meaning). A valid day has at least one non-null daily field; seven valid dates can still have partial individual values. No observed station data or forecast issue timestamps are invented.

**Frontend:** one `DailyForecast` component replaces the original three-day table. Responsive ordered cards show date/weekday, documented WMO condition, temperature minimum/maximum in °C and daily precipitation total in mm. Full, short, missing-field, wholly missing-row and empty forecast states are explicit; unknown codes are diagnostic. Existing loading, abort/late-response safety, provider errors, manual entry, map selection, GPS-by-action, reset and refresh remain. Seven-day series replaces old data when the point changes; no duplicated forecast section. Existing stylesheet, selection component, historical and drainage modules, Playwright configuration and their regression tests stay byte-identical. New CSS is scoped to daily cards.

**Seven-day semantics/source:** official https://open-meteo.com/en/docs inspected for forecast_days, daily variables, timezone, response coordinates and WMO codes. Each day is an Asia/Kolkata calendar day beginning today; today's total includes elapsed hours, not a future-only rolling 24 hours. The daily weather code is the most severe condition during that day. Model estimates/forecasts remain different from station observations. Retrieval is not issuance. Existing source/licence attribution retained; current weather forecasts are not automatically historical prediction-time training inputs.

**Actual production-browser demonstration:** two bounded real weather requests, default Bengaluru then manually selected verified existing Udupi OSM anchor, through the built React preview and FastAPI. ## 2026-10-08 — Application MVP Sprint 3: browser-session refresh and freshness complete

**Objective/status:** completed automatic six-hour selected-location weather refresh and explicit freshness states in the existing React → FastAPI → Open-Meteo application. No server scheduler, dependencies, ML, Stage 5B, flood predictions, drainage scores, emergency alerts, hydrological features or new research extraction. Flood prediction remains explicitly unavailable.

**Starting inspection:** branch `feature/karnataka-data-foundation`, previous independently verified commit `009a339fc9c32ba07344ca94dbb39d088f4f0582`, origin `git@github.com:Codovia/FloodThings.git`. Inspected API/adapters, retrieval/validity/daily schemas, dashboard/selection flow, current tests and unchanged Playwright configuration, README, source register, previous progress and protected manifests. Master/Codovia specification files remain absent; read preserved QandA §6 FR3/FR5 and update-frequency context as product intent, not implementation evidence. The present task explicitly scopes the six-hour cadence to browser-session weather, separate from legacy alert-sweep claims. Starting independent update.md whitespace and untracked QandA are preserved outside this checkpoint. No credential access or destructive Git operations.

**Implementation:** new `frontend/src/useWeatherSession.js` retains the existing fetch/parser behavior in a small session hook. Default Bengaluru and every new selected coordinate pair load immediately. Successful backend `retrieved_at` determines the next six-hour refresh. Exact coordinate-pair identity prevents rerenders/name changes/visibility bursts from scheduling duplicate calls; explicit same-point selection and manual refresh remain intentional retrieval actions. Only one request is active per selected session. Location changes abort old fetches, clear request timeouts and discard old data; stopped sessions and mismatched render keys cannot overwrite/display the new selection. Unmount removes timers/listeners and aborts requests.

**Scheduling:** one-shot timers recheck the wall clock; no polling service or global clock schedule. Automatic network requests pause while hidden/offline; visibility, focus and online events recheck eligibility without bypassing retry cooldowns. Visible offline data still ages to stale without making requests. The existing current-model 90-minute validity warning also ages independently of the retrieval freshness clock. Browser throttling/sleep can delay callbacks; reactivation catches overdue work. No refresh occurs while the application is closed, and there is no cross-tab or server scheduling claim. Consulted current primary React useEffect, MDN Page Visibility/setTimeout and Playwright Clock documentation: https://react.dev/reference/react/useEffect ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Page_Visibility_API ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/setTimeout ; https://playwright.dev/docs/clock .

**Freshness/UI:** a scoped desktop/mobile indicator reports fresh, stale, refreshing or unavailable separately from request errors. Fresh means a usable success with a verified retrieval clock less than six hours old. Stale means elapsed freshness, unknown retrieval age or a failed refresh retaining older same-location information. Refreshing identifies an active request and explicitly labels retained values as previously retrieved. Unavailable means no usable response exists. Missing, malformed, timezone-less or future retrieval clocks cannot establish freshness; no browser receipt is substituted as a backend success timestamp. Displayed UTC retrieval time is converted to IST separately from provider current valid time, original forecast dates and the still-null provider issuance timestamp. Seven actual calendar dates, units, source attribution and requested-versus-grid coordinates remain unchanged. The forecast note now names its response's starting date rather than claiming retained data always start today.

**Failures/retries:** backend behavior and API schema are unchanged: one upstream request, absolute 10-second provider deadline, invalid coordinates 422, provider/payload failures 503 without fallback readings. Browser timeout remains 15 seconds. Same-location refreshes retain usable values; failure shows an error and explicit stale label, leaving selection unchanged. Failed cycles allow at most three attempts: initial request, 30-minute retry, then 60-minute retry; automatic retries then pause until manual retry/new coordinates. Manual retry starts a new bounded cycle. Offline detection pauses requests and permits a due check on reconnection. A successful response without a verifiable retrieval timestamp waits six hours before another automatic attempt; an already-stale returned timestamp receives a 30-minute cooldown. No zero filling, estimates or fabricated dates/issuance times.

**Tests:** 22 new controlled-clock hook cases and 8 new isolated API timestamp/failure cases. Focused frontend refresh/location/forecast **41 passed**; full normal frontend **55/55 passed**, with the pre-existing Leaflet unit timing failure not reproduced in either normal full run. Focused backend weather/frozen-review group **93 passed**; full backend **1,188 passed**, one existing Starlette deprecation warning. Five new controlled-clock browser cases plus existing weather/GPS/map/historical/drainage cases: final unchanged normal Playwright configuration **13/13 passed**. Production Vite build passed (36 modules). No six-hour real wait; unit and browser clocks exercised the exact boundary, fresh activation, hidden/offline expiry, bounded retries, manual recovery, location changes during refresh, duplicate prevention, missing clocks and timer/listener cleanup. A previous same-location browser failure assertion was changed to require retained data plus explicit stale status because that behavior is the Sprint 3 requirement; new-location failure still requires clearing old readings. No historical/drainage tests, selectors, timeouts or configuration were weakened.

Initial draft tests exposed an unawaited React act callback and a test helper that lacked the default adapter's fetch method; corrected only the new fixture/helpers, then reran full suites. The first full browser run was 12 passed / 1 failed because the new indicator duplicated an existing 'Weather unavailable' locator; corrected production indicator wording, preserving the existing historical assertion, then all 13 passed. ## 2026-10-09 — Application MVP Sprint 6: PostgreSQL/PostGIS directory persistence

Native PostgreSQL **18.6** listens on port 5433 but current-user authentication fails (missing pioneer role; postgres peer authentication denied); PostGIS is not installed there. That service and unrelated running containers were untouched. Used an already cached official Docker PostGIS image in a newly created isolated project container `floodpulse-sprint6-db`, loopback-only port 55436, dedicated persistent volume `floodpulse_sprint6_data`, database `floodpulse_directory`. ## 9 October 2026 — Application MVP Sprint 7: working trained rainfall outlook

**Objective/status:** completed the bounded real-data trained-model demonstration, separately from unsupported flood-occurrence ML. Branch `feature/karnataka-data-foundation`; previous independently verified remote **18613b4b41d1c1b3bb6eb73387509ecb291ff53b** (Sprint 6). User explicitly redirected Sprint 7 from database hardening to an experimental next-24-hour heavy-rainfall target. No Stage 5B, flood classifier, drainage score, fabricated flood labels or alerts. Existing science/readiness decisions remain unchanged.

**Target/source:** at least **64.5 mm precipitation/24 hours**, including IMD heavier categories. IMD Climate Research heavy-rainfall page and 12 September 2023 press-release legend independently support the amount boundary; the official August 2019 deep-depression report page 17 documents the 08:30 IST reporting-day convention. This target is a rolling UTC model-grid proxy, explicitly different from that reporting day and ground-station rainfall. Two exact permitted directory-v2 points: Kundapur 13.6250993/74.6915722 and Mangaluru 12.8698101/74.8430082. No SOI or other restricted geometry used. Two bounded original Open-Meteo archive requests: `models=era5`, 2022-12-28 through 2026-01-01, UTC, hourly mm/°C/%/hPa and snowfall cm. ERA5 is retrospective 0.25-degree reanalysis, not station observation. Source grids 13.75/74.75 and 13.0/74.75; snowfall zero throughout both retained sources. Exact source URLs, original bytes, retrieval timestamps, licences and SHA-256 receipts retained locally and referenced in model metadata/source register. CC BY 4.0 data, free-service non-commercial terms; locality metadata retains OSM/ODbL attribution.

**Real training/features:** 8,768 complete examples with six-hour UTC cutoffs in 2023–2025; zero missing windows. Inputs: 24-hour and 72-hour precipitation sums, past-24-hour maximum hourly precipitation, past-24-hour mean temperature/humidity/surface pressure, seasonal sine/cosine. Input timestamps T-72h through T-1h, final precipitation interval ending T-1h; target intervals T through T+24h using provider preceding-hour accumulation stamps T+1h through T+24h. No rainfall at/after T enters inputs. Missing inputs/targets fail or are excluded explicitly, never zero-filled. Train labels end at/before 2025-01-01T00:00Z; holdout cutoffs start 2025-01-05T00:00Z, four-day embargo so held-out input windows do not overlap training target windows. **5,842 training rows (113 at/above threshold; 5,729 below)**; **2,888 held-out rows (96 at/above; 2,792 below)**; 38 embargo/split exclusions. These are rainfall classes, not flood-negative labels.

**Actual trained model/evaluation:** pinned scikit-learn 1.8.0 / NumPy 2.5.3 in the existing project-local environment. StandardScaler fitted to training only + LogisticRegression(C=1, class_weight=balanced, lbfgs, max_iter=1000, random_state=7), converged without holdout tuning. Held-out matrix **[[2273,519],[8,88]]** (TN/FP, FN/TP); precision **0.14497528830313014**, recall **0.9166666666666666**, F1 **0.2503556187766714**. Always-below baseline has zero positive-class precision/recall/F1. Low precision/519 false positives forbid warning claims; six-hour overlapping labels and nearby coastal grids are not independent event samples. No RF comparison, calibration, unsupported accuracy or live skill claim. Saved portable fitted scaler/learned coefficients in `backend/models/rainfall_logistic_v1/model.json`, SHA-256 **f3fa9b53d7047746f9d26cba8556705fb3fd2328128a236cb4f423575acf4316**; full provenance/metrics in metadata.json. Every holdout forward pass matches real sklearn. All source-to-table rows and exact deterministic retraining passed; three separate raw scalar sums independently matched historical 24/72-hour features and target totals. Detailed local CSV SHA-256 **a793a5f64ce81214a0df6fd73935b5510d0faee3d9b80278d9111b37579efaa8**. Original JSON responses 2,325,781 bytes combined (2,328,406 including receipts); detailed CSV 2,195,359 bytes and manifest 6,891 bytes remain local. No production fixture or manufactured training observation.

**Training/live compatibility:** identical variable names/units/feature algorithm are verified. ERA5 delayed reanalysis versus live Open-Meteo best-match model past-hours differs in grid, model version and publication semantics: **PROXY_REQUIRES_VALIDATION**. Historical reanalysis availability at issuance was not established. Thus past valid-time leakage checks do not imply as-issued forecasting skill. Live cutoffs may be other full UTC hours than training's six-hour grid. Only the two exact reviewed training points are supported; other selectable/manual/GPS/map points retain weather and get explicit model unavailable. No statewide extrapolation or station truth claim.

**API/frontend:** `GET /api/ai/rainfall-outlook?latitude=...&longitude=...` validates coordinates, loads the checksummed actual artifact, fetches four real past-hourly variables with `past_hours=74`, `forecast_hours=1`, UTC/mm/celsius, validates grid/units/completeness and consumes no future values. One ten-second bounded request, no automatic retry. Output includes binary experimental result, model/version/hash, requested point/provider grid, input features/units/checksum, successful UTC retrieval and a 24-hour horizon beginning at the next full UTC hour (within one hour of retrieval). Provider issuance remains null; probability is deliberately null/un-calibrated. Invalid coordinates 422; unsupported scope/missing/incompatible inputs/provider failure 503 with no replacement prediction. Small accessible on-demand AI Rainfall Outlook panel preserves existing dashboard and warns about the proxy/limited precision. Once opened, it requests updated inputs after a new successful selected-location weather receipt. It does not duplicate the seven-day request; its separate hourly request supplies otherwise unavailable model features. Location-change/unmount cancellation prevents late responses, timeouts/errors clear predictions, manual retry is available, and stale weather explicitly labels a previously retrieved outlook. PostgreSQL selection, manual/GPS/map inputs, seven-day weather, six-hour browser cadence and historical/drainage maps preserved.

**Actual live browser demonstration:** production Vite build + FastAPI using the persistent PostgreSQL directory through the existing SELECT-only reader role. Default Bengaluru followed by Kundapur then Mangaluru: exactly **three real weather requests plus two real AI hourly-input requests**, all successful; no API fixtures. Both outlooks **below 64.5 mm**, with distinct genuine feature vectors; an independent JavaScript forward pass from saved scaler/coefficients exactly matches both classes. No forced difference or fabricated inference. Backend model artifact was actually loaded, all real features passed to it, model/version/retrieval/windows displayed correctly. Seven genuine forecast dates 9–15 October 2026, point/grid metadata, fresh state, selected headings and centered Leaflet markers verified; mobile no overflow, zero page errors. Desktop/mobile screenshots visually inspected and complete original responses/receipts retained locally. External browser basemap tiles blocked to bound traffic; backend Open-Meteo calls were real. A separate initial direct Kundapur live inference also passed. Temporary demo servers stopped; existing persistent DB retained. Demonstration: select Kundapur, wait for weather, run model; select Dakshina Kannada → Mangaluru and see fresh selected-location inference.

**Tests/verification:** focused backend **28/28** including real retained-source retraining and all holdout predictions; focused frontend **13/13**; full backend **1,267/1,267** (one pre-existing Starlette deprecation warning), frontend **83/83**, PostgreSQL-backed Playwright **22/22**, production build **passed (39 modules)**. Initial frontend **82 passed/1 failed** exposed duplicate Open-Meteo link accessible names introduced by the new panel; fixed the new link name, unchanged existing test then passed. First browser run **19 passed/3 failed**: two new fixture errors due to an unpadded forecast day, plus an existing directory loading timeout under concurrent validation load. Corrected only new fixture ISO formatting; all original assertions/configuration unchanged, full final normal run 22/22. No Leaflet assertion/configuration was weakened. Live integration separate from explicit offline browser fixtures. All **48** existing offline scientific/frozen-review/directory source validators passed; new training reproduction validator passed separately. Tests never train on synthetic fixtures or make normal unit-test live requests.

**Protected evidence:** **1,967/1,967** starting protected files match exact bytes/SHA-256/size/nanosecond mtime, zero exceptions. Starting inventory 1,973; exactly six existing authorized code/document files excluded (README, main API, requirements, App, source register, update.md). Annual CHIRPS remains **11,315 rows/365 dates/31 IDs**, SHA-256 **b67ad94d07e4e633b286e7c11015fef98fee31e2601d475985a9568741728819**. GFD **33/23/2/329**, measured hydrology **1,697/81/14/33** unchanged. Existing directory v1/v2, raw sources, SOI/CWC/GloFAS/rainfall evidence and previous recovery files preserved. QandA remains untracked/unchanged; unrelated starting update.md blank-line edits remain outside the reviewed index.

**Files/delivery:** added shared past-only feature module, trained-artifact loader/inference, immutable bounded training/reproduction script, JSON model/provenance, focused backend/frontend/browser tests and the small React panel; updated existing API, requirements, App, README and maintained source register. This chronological entry precedes the reviewed commit. Publish only reviewed text code, permitted learned parameters/checksum/provenance and this append; raw responses/detailed CSV/screenshots/logs, secrets, restricted sources and QandA excluded. Commit/push and fresh `git ls-remote` equality are reported only after execution, not claimed here. Record this checkpoint hash in a future task rather than an extra self-referential commit.

## 9 October 2026 — Application MVP Sprint 8: administrator-controlled emergency shelters

**Objective/checkpoint:** complete a minimal authorized administrator → manually verified shelter assignment → existing PostgreSQL/PostGIS → public map → external entrance directions workflow. Starting branch `feature/karnataka-data-foundation`, independently verified predecessor `e9f3016d447374435ba202e87d391a262f28c9bf`. Implementation and isolated end-to-end demonstration completed; genuine operational shelter coverage remains **zero**, deliberately. This is an unaffiliated project prototype. No historical shelter listing was validated retrospectively, imported or activated. No ML retraining of the deployed artifact, Stage 5B, locality expansion or hydrological research was started.

**Authentication/security:** separate `/admin` React interface and protected FastAPI login/session/logout/write/audit endpoints; no public registration or default credentials. Explicit maintenance provisioning uses non-echoing `getpass` confirmation, 16–128-character passwords, salted memory-hard scrypt N=131072/r=8/p=1, and explicit reset that revokes sessions. Bounded Argon2 package access failed, so the existing Python/OpenSSL implementation uses the current OWASP-supported scrypt alternative without dependency changes. Random opaque HttpOnly/SameSite=Strict session cookies; only token hashes in PostgreSQL. Eight-hour absolute and 30-minute idle expiry, logout revocation, exact browser-Origin validation, custom same-origin JSON login header and per-session CSRF write header. Production Secure cookies require HTTPS; insecure cookies are allowed only when explicitly configured for loopback development. Login attempts persist in bounded account/client-address buckets (5/20 per 15-minute window); simultaneous scrypt work is bounded. No administrator password, token or hash is printed, included in error bodies, stored in React localStorage or committed. Admin/availability responses are no-store; frame-denial/nosniff/referrer headers cover API and Vite dev/preview. Deployment must supply TLS and equivalent static-host headers. Forwarded client addresses are not trusted; a proxy may share the peer rate limit.

**Database/migration:** reused the existing loopback PostgreSQL **16.4** / PostGIS **3.4.3** directory database. Applied Alembic `0002_emergency_shelters` after `0001_location_directory`; five new shelter-only tables: administrators, sessions, login limits, assignments and audit. WGS84 entrance POINT with **X=longitude/Y=latitude**, coordinate equality/SRID checks, capacity/occupancy/state/verification constraints, district-version foreign keys, spatial/availability/history indexes. Directory records and existing tables were compared before/after migration and preserved. A separate least-privilege shelter-service login can read district identities/administrator hashes, manage sessions and assignments, and append audit history; it cannot alter administrators/location records, delete shelters or modify/delete audits. Maintenance credentials are excluded from the API environment. Real isolated PostgreSQL tests verify clean/existing-schema migrations, rollback, constraints, authentication and restricted-role access; SQLite is not used.

**Manual workflow:** responsive administrator login/logout, paginated list, create Pending assignment, edit address/district/verified entrance via Leaflet or coordinates, capacity/occupancy, water/toilets/accessibility, approved public contact, restrictions and private notes. Creation cannot directly publish an Open record. Open/Full updates require renewed explicit confirmations of facility authorization, exact entrance, present usability and capacity, plus verification evidence; form confirmations reset after loading/saving. Open requires spare capacity; Full requires occupancy equal to maximum. Closed preserves the previous verification timestamp as historical context; Pending clears active verification. Every assignment change and before/after status/capacity snapshot is audited in the same transaction; audit failure rolls back the assignment. Revision conflicts return 409. Verification/update times and audit history are visible to staff. NIC district identities are retained with unresolved independent current-LGD reconciliation, rather than fabricated official codes.

**Public directory/navigation:** on-demand `GET /api/shelters` returns only verified Open records with spare capacity and verification younger than **24 hours**. This is an application expiry policy, not an official standard or occupancy guarantee. Full/Closed/Pending/expired/demo records are excluded from live destinations. Empty state is exactly “No currently verified open shelters are available in this directory.” Selected point supplies optional approximate PostGIS straight-line distance, explicitly distinct from road distance. Leaflet displays stored entrance points; encoded Google Maps directions use that exact destination and optional selected origin, following official Maps URLs documentation. Routes are not verified flood-safe, and users must confirm access/availability before travel. Contact details require disclosure approval; internal notes, evidence and administrator identities are private. Location switching aborts/suppresses late directory responses; timeouts/database failures clear destinations without replacements or retry loops; expiry removes stale availability. Existing weather/GPS/manual/map/AI requests are not coupled to assignment creation and never trigger alerts.

**Actual browser demonstration:** production React build → authenticated FastAPI using the restricted application role → separate UUID-named PostgreSQL/PostGIS demonstration database → public citizen map. No API fixtures in this demonstration; one real existing default Open-Meteo weather request succeeded. An unmistakably labelled isolated test facility progressed **Pending → Open → Full → Closed → Open**, with five durable audit events and no public Pending/Full/Closed destination. Administrator map placement updated coordinates; the test entrance `13.5,74.7` produced the exact encoded navigation destination, two origin/entrance markers and no private contact/notes leakage. Unauthenticated writes were rejected (401), logout revoked the session, desktop/mobile screenshots were inspected, zero page errors and no horizontal overflow. These are test coordinates and confirmations, not genuine emergency assignments. The entire disposable database was removed after verification. Task-created temporary administrator accounts/password files were removed; the live database has **0 shelters, 0 shelter audit entries and 0 operational administrators** until designated staff privately provision their own account. Existing directory remains 31 districts / 6 locality records / 5 selectable localities. No fake operational availability survives the demonstration.

**Tests/results:** focused offline backend **29/29**; full backend **1,296/1,296** (only the existing Starlette TestClient deprecation warning). Real PostgreSQL integration **37/37**, comprising 19 existing directory tests and 18 new shelter cases, zero skips. New frontend **15/15**; full suite final **98/98**. ## 2026-10-10 — Application MVP Sprint 9: administrator-approved Telegram notifications

**Objective/status:** implemented the bounded `/admin` compose → exact server preview → explicit approval → one Telegram attempt → PostgreSQL history workflow. This is an application feature, not automatic flood forecasting or a new research/ML sprint. **Live delivery verification is BLOCKED:** `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` and authorized destination configuration are absent. No real Telegram request or operational notice was sent; isolated fixtures are not described as live acceptance. Previous independently verified checkpoint **`c766617df67ac82566810d1a9190b574535458e2`**, branch `feature/karnataka-data-foundation`; `git ls-remote` confirmed that starting remote hash during this task.

**Architecture/configuration:** added `telegram_delivery.py`, protected notification routes/store and `AdminNotifications.jsx` within the existing authenticated admin page. Bot token and alias-to-destination JSON are backend environment configuration only. Destination aliases/labels are authenticated metadata; actual chat IDs/token are excluded from API responses and database rows. Configuration validates numeric destinations, unique destination labels/IDs, explicit private test routing, and credentials-like content. Missing/invalid configuration disables the composer, without automatic network calls or fake outcomes. Telegram's current official Bot API `sendMessage` documentation was reviewed at `https://core.telegram.org/bots/api#sendmessage` on 10 October 2026: HTTPS JSON, returned Message on acceptance and the documented text limit. A single TLS-verified stdlib HTTPS POST has 10-second connection/socket timeouts, bounded response bytes, no redirects/proxies/retries and no token-bearing URL logging. No new scheduling service, dependencies, email or subscriber interface was introduced.

**Administrator workflow/security:** existing opaque HttpOnly/Secure/SameSite-strict sessions, scrypt passwords, login abuse controls, same-origin checks, CSRF and no-store responses are reused. Only the composing administrator may approve the stored message hash. Review creates an unsent immutable preview; changing text/type/destination/shelter resets browser approval. Previews expire after 30 minutes. A token/chat fingerprint and label bind the approval to the original configured recipient. Optional shelter text is built from a currently verified Open PostgreSQL record, excludes private notes/contact data, and is rechecked before send; changed/expired shelter data requires a new review. Demonstration mode permits only explicitly designated private test destinations. No experimental rainfall output triggers a message; no flood probability, fabricated shelter availability or automated evacuation instruction is generated. Administrators remain responsible for independently verifying all manually written claims. No authority affiliation is claimed.

**Database/API:** additive Alembic revision **`0003_admin_notifications`**, following unchanged `0002_emergency_shelters`, adds `admin_notifications` with UUID identities/idempotency key, administrator foreign keys, exact message/hash, destination alias/label/fingerprint, optional public shelter snapshot, environment separation, UTC creation/confirmation/attempt/result times and sanitized result/error/message identifiers. Appropriate constraints and a history index preserve approval/status integrity. Endpoints: authenticated `GET /api/admin/notifications/options`, bounded `GET /api/admin/notifications?limit=50&offset=0`, `POST /api/admin/notifications` (preview only) and `POST /api/admin/notifications/{id}/send` (explicit approval plus exact hash). Migration ran on established local PostgreSQL **16.4 / PostGIS 3.4.3**, with every existing row in all ten directory/shelter tables unchanged by migration. Granted only SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE on the new table to the existing limited application role. Production notification table remains empty; no administrator or shelter fixture was provisioned into it. Real integration tests create/drop only an isolated UUID-named PostgreSQL database and temporary test role.

**Duplicate/failure semantics:** same-key preview retries return the original record; changed content with a reused key is rejected. A locked transaction durably changes Draft → Sending before external I/O, so repeated/concurrent approvals never repeat the Telegram attempt. Recorded outcomes are draft, sending, accepted, rejected and delivery_unknown. Accepted means the API returned a verified matching Message; it does not prove recipients read it. A timeout/unverifiable response remains uncertain with no automatic resend. A final database-write failure or crash can leave Sending; the UI requires inspection rather than pretending delivery failed. Browser controls remove the repeat-send action immediately, including when the HTTP response is lost. At-most-one attempt per notification does not guarantee exactly-once delivery. Refreshing history never sends a notice.

**Frontend/accessibility:** compact labelled destination/type/message/shelter controls, exact plain-text preview, explicit confirmation checkbox, busy/result indicators and paginated history. Existing responsive shelter styling and native keyboard controls are reused. Tests cover keyboard confirmation, mobile width, disabled/unconfigured states, content changes invalidating approval, lost responses and no repeated-send action. Shelter management, directory/map/GPS/manual location selection, seven-day weather, six-hour freshness, existing experimental rainfall outlook and historical GIS remain intact. No production mock mode was added.

**Tests and browser demonstration:** focused offline notification **31 passed**, within final full backend **1,327 passed**; one existing Starlette deprecation warning. Focused real PostgreSQL notification **12 passed**, full directory/shelter/notification PostgreSQL suite **49 passed**, plus final explicit send-CSRF verification passed. New frontend **9 passed**, full unchanged frontend **107 passed**. New Playwright notification **3 passed** within final full unchanged browser configuration **29 passed**. Production build passed (**44 modules**). Browser fixtures are visibly labelled DEMONSTRATION ONLY and do not contact Telegram: sign in with isolated fixture → choose private test destination → compose → review → keyboard-confirm → one simulated acceptance with recipient-reading caveat → inspect history/logout; mobile missing-configuration and uncertain-delivery cases also pass. Real authenticated TestClient/PostgreSQL tests separately verify actual record persistence, exact preview approval, concurrency, CSRF, rollback, least privilege, missing configuration, Telegram rejection/timeout and shelter snapshot changes. There is no real delivery claim.

**Failures investigated:** initial cold PostGIS installation in the disposable test database exceeded the existing five-second statement timeout; later unchanged clean/full migration tests passed. Initial notification integration found a genuine optional JSONB snapshot issue (JSON null versus SQL NULL); corrected without weakening constraints. A new shelter fixture was corrected to renew the already required Open confirmations. Initial browser run had 26 passed/3 failed: two unchanged data/map loading timing failures under concurrent validation load, and one new notification test using an ambiguous global alert locator. The new locator now scopes to the actual notification region; final unchanged existing assertions/configuration passed all 29 without concurrent PostgreSQL test load. No existing tests were edited to hide failures; the existing migration-head assertion was updated solely for the new legitimate Alembic head.

**Protected-data verification:** **2,070/2,070 protected starting files** preserve bytes/SHA-256/size/nanosecond mtime, no exceptions, excluding exactly seven authorized existing application/document files. Annual CHIRPS remains **11,315 rows/365 dates/31 identifiers**, SHA-256 `b67ad94d07e4e633b286e7c11015fef98fee31e2601d475985a9568741728819`. GFD **33/23/2/329** and measured hydrology **1,697/81/14/33** remain unchanged, as do all Sentinel-1, rainfall, GloFAS, scientific decisions, restricted geography and directory sources. Saved ML model SHA-256 remains `f3fa9b53d7047746f9d26cba8556705fb3fd2328128a236cb4f423575acf4316`; no saved or deployed model was regenerated or replaced. All **49 existing scientific/source/model reproducibility checks passed** (46 research/frozen-review checks plus both public-directory validators and the existing saved-model validator). The latter recomputes the original fit in memory solely to verify exact saved coefficients; no weights, model metadata, dataset or deployed artifact were written. The scripts environment completed the 46 research checks; the remaining application validators used the backend environment because the scripts environment lacks FastAPI. No validator was weakened or source regenerated. ## 2026-10-10: UI Rendering Investigation & Repair — Five Pages & Multi-Viewport Audit

**Role & Objective:** Conducted a comprehensive frontend visual inspection, reproduction, and repair of UI rendering defects across the five FloodPulse pages (`/`, `/weather`, `/flood-map`, `/shelters`, `/admin`) at five specified viewports (Desktop 1920×1080, Laptop 1366×768, Tablet 768×1024, Mobile 390×844, and Small Mobile 360×740). Maintained strict separation between actual rendering defects and legitimate unavailable data (e.g. 0 verified hazard polygons, unverified navigation bounds for Kodagu, and empty operational shelter directories).

**Phase 1 & 2 — Diagnosis & Automated Audit Harness:**
- Inspected running processes: FastAPI backend on port 18050, Vite dev server on port 14180, PostgreSQL directory container on port 55436.
- Implemented an automated Playwright audit harness (`data/tmp/ui-evidence/audit.mjs`, stored in git-ignored directory) capturing full-page and viewport screenshots and measuring horizontal overflow, clipped interactive elements, Leaflet tile loading, and console errors across all 5 routes and 5 target viewports.
- Baseline ("before") run captured 30+ screenshots (`data/tmp/ui-evidence/before/`) and uncovered specific visual flaws:
  1. *Weather coordinate form and point selector layout:* On `/weather`, the coordinate inputs and submit button were unconstrained and stretched in a single row without a 2-column grid; the map rendered below rather than side-by-side. On `/` and `/shelters`, toggling "Hide weather map" left a blank 63% whitespace column due to unconditional 2-column grid styling on `.point-selector-body`.
  2. *Map legend swatch floating on mobile:* On narrow mobile viewports (390×844 and 360×740), multi-line legend text wrapped across 4–5 lines while `.map-legend` used `align-items: center`, causing the historical water swatch to float vertically in the middle of the text block rather than aligning with the top line.
  3. *Desktop Admin Login Form Over-Stretching:* On wide desktop viewports (1920×1080 and 1366×768), `.admin-shell` had `max-width: none` without capping the login card width, causing the username and password inputs to span 1200+ px.
  4. *Mobile navigation menu dismissal:* Clicking the currently active route or link inside the expanded mobile header menu failed to close the menu because `PageLink` did not invoke passed `onClick` handlers.
  5. *Leaflet CSS import & container resilience:* `FloodIntelligenceMap.jsx` lacked an explicit import of `leaflet/dist/leaflet.css`, and `DrainageResearchLayers.jsx` did not integrate the `useMapResize` hook for window resize recalculation. In addition, `history?.geojson.features.length` lacked optional chaining.

**Phase 3 — Targeted Repairs Applied:**
- `frontend/src/styles.css`:
  - Scoped 2-column point-selector layout to `.point-selector-body:has(.point-map)` so that collapsing the weather map gracefully collapses the container to single-column width without orphaned whitespace.
  - Made `.weather-coordinate-form` globally styled as a 2-column grid (`repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr))`) with button spanning full width (`grid-column: 1 / -1`), collapsing to single-column below 500px across all routes.
  - Updated `.map-legend` to `align-items: flex-start` with `.water-swatch` given `margin-top: 4px` and `flex-shrink: 0`, keeping swatches crisply aligned to the first line of wrapped text.
  - Added `.admin-shell .shelter-form:not([aria-label]) { max-width: 560px; }` to constrain unauthenticated login cards on desktop.
- `frontend/src/AppNavigation.jsx`:
  - Updated `PageLink` to preserve and trigger `props.onClick` on link clicks.
  - Added `onClick={() => setExpanded(false)}` to navigation links in `AppHeader` to dismiss the mobile menu upon navigation.
- `frontend/src/FloodIntelligenceMap.jsx`:
  - Added explicit `import 'leaflet/dist/leaflet.css'`.
  - Added safe optional chaining (`history?.geojson?.features?.length`) for historical geometry feature checks.
- `frontend/src/DrainageResearchLayers.jsx`:
  - Imported and called `useMapResize(map)` hook in `ResearchMap` to guarantee Leaflet tile invalidation upon layout or container resizing.
- `frontend/playwright.config.js`:
  - Preserved `...process.env` while configuring test-isolated `SHELTER_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` and `SHELTER_COOKIE_SECURE` for clean test execution.

**Phase 4 — Verification & Evidence:**
- Re-ran the audit harness (`data/tmp/ui-evidence/audit.mjs after`), capturing 30+ after screenshots (`data/tmp/ui-evidence/after/`) and report metrics:
  - Horizontal overflow: `overflowX = false` across all 5 viewports and all 5 routes (document width matches viewport width exactly).
  - Leaflet navigation: In-app routing, district switching (Udupi, Dakshina Kannada, Kodagu, statewide), and browser back/forward history transitions verified cleanly with full tile rendering and 0 console errors.
  - Visual inspection: Confirmed balanced weather coordinate layout, constrained desktop admin login card, top-aligned mobile legend swatch, and responsive mobile menu dismissal.
- Full automated test suite passes:
  - Vitest: 14 test files, 135/135 tests passing (`npm test`).
  - Playwright: 35/35 integration tests passing with private launcher environment (`npx playwright test`).
  - Pytest: 1,354/1,354 backend tests passing (`pytest backend/tests`).
  - Vite production build: 48 modules cleanly bundled with zero errors (`npm run build`).


## 2026-10-10: UI and Map Repair — policy-compliant tile identification and page composition

**Objective/start:** repair the reported external-Chrome OSM 403 and repetitive location-form layouts, starting from `254a50c2db459e40ec11291431e6b8c0bd9a8c67` on `feature/karnataka-data-foundation`. Confirmed Antigravity's prior resize, Leaflet CSS, mobile navigation, legend and admin form fixes are present and retained. Preserve unrelated `backend/.env.example` and untracked `QandA.md` exactly; no backend, database, model, source dataset or scientific-methodology edits.

**Tile diagnosis/source review:** Vite's global `Referrer-Policy: same-origin` suppressed the Referer on cross-origin Leaflet tile images. Actual installed Google Chrome 154.0.8037.57 before-audit requests had no Referer, a confirmed violation of OSMF's web-tile identification policy (https://operations.osmfoundation.org/policies/tiles/, reviewed 10 October 2026). The user's reported 403 did not reproduce on this network: before audit returned 99 HTTP 200 tile responses. Therefore the exact original server-side block reason remains unverified; the missing-referrer defect is a plausible cause, not proof of a particular ban. Bounded alternative review: CARTO's current official raster service requires a key (https://docs.carto.com/faqs/carto-basemaps); OSM France terms exclude private/login/intranet use (https://www.openstreetmap.fr/usage/). Other community documentation was inaccessible or insufficient to verify applicability. No registration, paid service or unreviewed provider switch.

**Map repair:** centralized all five existing Leaflet basemaps in `mapBasemap.js`; retained standard `https://tile.openstreetmap.org/{z}/{x}/{y}.png`, visible copyright attribution, ordinary browser caching and genuine browser identification. Each image sends only the actual app origin via `referrerPolicy: origin`, while the global HTML privacy header and all backend security headers remain unchanged. No private route/query/credential in the referrer; no spoofing, proxy, rotation, bulk/offline acquisition or cache bypass. First tile failure removes only the background layer with explicit unavailable notice; existing polygons/markers and coordinate selection remain usable, without retry loops/provider substitution. `VITE_MAP_BASEMAP=none` explicitly disables tile requests; unknown settings also fail closed. Documented in README and `docs/MAP_TILE_POLICY.md`; ignored local configuration only, no secrets.

**Page composition:** Home now pairs a prominent Karnataka point-map and current verified shelter summary with compact seven-day weather and experimental-AI cards. The shared location controls are collapsed behind keyboard-accessible “Change location.” Weather & AI retains full seven-day cards/chart, model interface, freshness and source timestamps, with an optional point map. Shelters prioritizes the verified directory/empty state with compact location selection rather than a repeated weather form. Flood Map retains the large map, all 31 district choices, original historical polygons, independent unavailable hazard layer, drainage, legend and evidence details. Admin retains its existing bounded layout, protected login, shelter verification and explicit Telegram approval. Shared location/weather/AI components stay mounted across page navigation; no duplicated weather request or freshness lifecycle. GPS/manual/map selection, requested/grid coordinates, seven-day local-calendar semantics and six-hour stale/error behavior remain intact. No fabricated shelter, risk, probability, alert or flood prediction.

**Independent browser evidence:** all five routes audited before/after at 1366×768, 1920×1080 and 390×844 using installed Google Chrome via Playwright/DevTools in headless browser contexts, independent of the IDE browser and without provider fixtures. The user's exact Chrome profile/network was not reproduced. After audit: 15 views, zero horizontal overflow, zero page exceptions, visible decoded map tiles and 75 HTTP 200 tile responses carrying `Referer: http://127.0.0.1:14180/`. Actual PostgreSQL-backed Kundapur and Mangaluru selections each returned HTTP 200 Open-Meteo weather/seven dates and HTTP 200 saved `rainfall_logistic_v1` inference; probability remains null. **Target and experiment proposed, not independently approved:** Target A: instantaneous measured stage in metres at a verified dated gauge, issue00/06/12/18UTC, +6h, most recent accepted input at most1h old and demonstrably available by issue. It is a river-variable forecast, not automatically flood prediction. Target B separately predicts strict exceedance of a compatible effective official CWC danger level at the same future instant. Equality/non-exceedance at a point never establishes absence throughout a6h interval or inundation elsewhere. Unknown/rejected/computed/conflicted/missing evidence stays UNKNOWN. Provisional common-history train2013June–2017June, validation2017June–2018June, independent test2018June–2020June; nominal periods not a sample-adequacy claim. ## 2026-10-13 — Home Emergency Dashboard (Home only)

**Scope:** Implemented the approved sketch 01 citizen dashboard on `/` only. Added HomeEmergencyDashboard, HomeFloodMap, Home-scoped CSS, strict summary/evidence helpers, a bounded Home geolocation hook and focused tests. App retains one weather session and separate GPS/weather point, locality, district filter and historical-cell identities. Other four pages retain their existing workflows; a minimal GPS source tag in the opt-in location selector preserves identity across routes. No backend, schema, model, operational flood-map layers, admin security or Telegram workflow changes.

**Location and interface:** Immediate Karnataka statewide context; one automatic secure-context getCurrentPosition request per tab session, 10-second browser/independent timeouts, recent timestamp and finite accuracy validation. Manual selection wins over late GPS; leaving Home invalidates pending callbacks. Denied, timed-out, unsupported, insecure and invalid fixes retain name search and statewide fallback. No watcher or GPS coordinate storage/public URL. Only a non-location attempt flag persists in sessionStorage. Outside-state points (including Goa) and boundary uncertainty are explained without an invented district. The compact location bar and three summaries sit above a 40% citizen-information / 60% interactive map grid. Home alone uses normal scrolling to prevent the inherited fixed workspace from clipping its large map; mobile stacks with no horizontal overflow, tested down to 320 px.

**Truthful summaries and overlays:** Current weather is a timestamped fresh Open-Meteo model estimate, never a station observation; daily condition codes are not current conditions. The rainfall helper sums only complete compatible mm intervals over an explicitly timestamped 24-hour future window beginning at the next complete IST hour. Daily-only API totals can cover that window only at a midnight alignment; other windows are unavailable, with no prorating, missing-hour substitution or second weather fetch. Exact flood wording: “Validated flood forecast unavailable for this location.” Warning feeds remain Not connected; no verified hazard or forecast polygons are invented, and missing coverage is not absence of risk. Home draws the existing 56 eligible historical Udupi observation cells (two GFD event-window products, not 56 floods) with provenance checks and detail links. One freshness-checked shelter receipt supplies list/map; only live, verified Open entrances with capacity qualify. Expiry/revalidation/failure remove destinations; demonstration fixtures are excluded. Actual directory is live and empty. Existing Shelters directions safeguards remain.

**State geography rights:** A bounded 5,103,747-byte geoBoundaries gbOpen India ADM1 simplified transfer was retained ignored and verified against publisher Git LFS SHA-256 4c63fe43294a391e8f2de4e9f86f3edb60f8688275b9fee90c61fb2aa0c26061. Only the 189,831-byte Karnataka IN-KA feature and source receipt are published, with unchanged coordinates, source attribution and CC BY 2.5 IN. Source is geoBoundaries/DataMeet/Election Commission of India, represented 2011, pinned commit 9469f09; it is context, not certified current administrative identity or flood geometry. A 2 km application caution margin plus browser accuracy marks border coverage uncertain; this is not source positional accuracy. No restricted SOI geometry/derivatives are redistributed and the existing public directory is unchanged.

**Actual validation:** Complete frontend 222 passed in 22 files (39.97 s); final focused Home/GPS checks 23 passed. Complete Playwright 60 passed (1.6 min), including native browser permission grant with controlled GPS coordinates, with controlled GPS/weather/shelter fixtures and real local PostgreSQL/read-only historical routes. Backend 1,353 passed, 1 model-training reproduction test deselected, 1 existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning (63.42 s); no training performed. All 24 retained source/provenance validators passed, including Phase 12G history validation. Production build passed; Vite reports the >500 kB bundle advisory. git diff --check passed. The existing configured PostgreSQL/PostGIS container was initially stopped and started for read-only runtime verification without creation, migrations or production record changes.

**Independent installed Chrome:** Google Chrome 154.0.8037.57, all five routes visited, no API mocking and no page errors. Measured Home Leaflet rectangles: 1366×768 viewport → 746×468 px; 1920×1080 → 1078.39×780 px; 390×844 → 332×380 px. All three had no horizontal overflow. Actual full-page screenshots and read-only receipts are in ignored data/recovery/home_emergency_v1/chrome-{1366x768,1920x1080,390x844}.png and chrome_verification.json. Native headless GPS fallback is distinct from controlled permission-grant tests and does not validate device GPS hardware. Real Kundapur weather returned HTTP 200 / 26.6 °C before midnight; the later real provider check returned HTTP 503 with “Weather provider returned incomplete or invalid data,” correctly displayed unavailable. Underlying provider failure cause is unconfirmed; no fallback weather was invented. Real shelter directory returned HTTP 200, live, zero records.

**Integrity exception and correction:** Baseline covers 3,225 existing files. The initial full browser suite's pre-existing isolated Admin screenshot test overwrote three ignored PNG fixtures under data/recovery/wireframe_ui_v1/after/admin-isolated-{1366,1920,390}.png. Original matching copies were not found; these fixture artifacts are regenerated, not falsely reported unchanged. The test now writes to Playwright's temporary output directory. They are not scientific observations and are not staged. All original research datasets, source manifests, scientific provenance, models, application assets outside approved edits, backend/.env.example and untracked QandA.md are verified separately against baseline bytes/SHA-256/mtime. Final protected receipt verifies 3,211 other protected files unchanged in bytes/SHA-256/mtime and records the three screenshot exceptions explicitly; 11 existing files have authorized code/documentation edits. Root update.md retains its exact previous byte prefix.

**Decision:** Citizen Home interface implemented; actual future-flood prediction and general next-24-hour rainfall coverage remain source-limited. Previous scientific NO-GO remains unchanged. See docs/HOME_EMERGENCY_DASHBOARD.md for implementation, licensing, real versus controlled checks and remaining dependencies.


## 2026-10-13 — Home viewport fit at 16:9 desktop sizes

**Request and scope:** Corrected the prior ~1366×1387 tall Home layout to fit an actual 1366×768 browser window. Home only: HomeEmergencyDashboard JSX/CSS, HomeFloodMap's toolbar/notes/initial framing and a Home-only viewport sizing hook. Branding/navigation remain, with compact Home header spacing. Weather & AI, Flood Map, Shelters, Admin, backend code, models, data sources and scientific claims remain unchanged.

**Layout:** Home-scoped 100vh fallback / 100dvh flex and grid sizing accounts for actual header/footer and row heights; min-height: 0 enables shrinkable workspace children. Location and three summary cards are compact; information/map widths remain 40%/60%. Essential flood-forecast unavailability and official warning status are outside the internally scrolling lower information region. That region is keyboard-focusable, scrolls only when needed, and retains weather/history/shelter detail links. Long source/freshness text uses accessible native details. No global body overflow suppression or tiny-font workaround. A ResizeObserver measures essential content and reserved rows; narrow/short/zoom-equivalent viewports or enlarged text that cannot fit retain natural scrolling. Mobile remains naturally scrollable. Map background failure notices do not collapse the canvas; Leaflet invalidates size through the existing hook and refreshes initial statewide framing after the first fit without moving a selected point. GPS, rainfall aggregation, source validity, historical filtering and shelter freshness behavior are preserved.

Document widths equal viewport widths, with no horizontal overflow. At primary acceptance size header, location bar, all three cards, both panels, complete map and footer are inside the viewport. The left essential section stays fixed while its lower region scrolls.

Desktop full-page image heights equal viewport heights; mobile full-page image is taller. Controlled browser fixtures are separate from actual Chrome captures.
