## 13 October 2026 — Home current weather and full-hour precipitation repair

**Baseline/scope:** Continued feature/karnataka-data-foundation from verified 8e26fa965338eeff5e84847efa733c96f38e077d. Repaired the existing shared FastAPI/Open-Meteo weather lifecycle and necessary Home presentation; retained all five routes and Weather & AI's seven-day contract. Rainfall model, flood model decisions, database/schema/records, operational Flood Map, shelters, Admin and Telegram implementations are unchanged. Unrelated backend/.env.example edits and untracked QandA.md remain outside staging.

**Diagnosis before editing:** Reviewed adapter, validators, point endpoint, shared browser cache/location lifecycle, Home and Weather & AI consumers, tests and Home documentation. Used retained reviewed Kundapur locality udupi-admin:municipality:kundapur (13.6250993,74.6915722), not guessed coordinates. The original official provider request returned HTTP 200 / 892-byte JSON and passed the original parser; the unchanged internal endpoint also returned HTTP 200. Extended documented request returned HTTP 200 / 5108-byte JSON, all five non-null current variables, seven daily rows and 168 hourly precipitation entries. Returned units: °C, %, mm, wmo code, km/h; timestamps iso8601, current interval seconds (900), timezone offset 19800. No legacy/current-name mismatch or upstream/intermediary outage was reproduced. The old retained 503 said “Weather provider returned incomplete or invalid data”; old handler inspection locates it in JSON-decoding/validation, so FastAPI generated that 503. The raw old upstream response and precise exception were not retained: exact offending field and underlying origin remain unresolved. No hypothetical midnight/cache cause is presented as established.

**Request/contract:** One bounded ten-second provider call for default/selected coordinates now requests current=temperature_2m,relative_humidity_2m,precipitation,weather_code,wind_speed_10m; hourly=precipitation; daily=precipitation_sum,temperature_2m_max,temperature_2m_min,weather_code; timezone=Asia/Kolkata; forecast_days=7; temperature_unit=celsius; precipitation_unit=mm. Default ISO timestamps are normalized with explicitly validated IST offset. No new source/model or second Home fetch. Present current values require compatible units/ranges; omitted/null optional fields retain other valid readings and per-field status. Current condition uses current WMO code, independent of daily severe-condition codes. Wind retains returned units; current precipitation retains its actual preceding interval and explicit endpoints. A missing/invalid hourly series reports its own reason without erasing valid current/daily data.

**Hourly semantics:** Pure deterministic assessNext24Rainfall(data, referenceMs) starts at the next full IST hour (an exact-hour reference starts there), requires exactly 24 consecutive one-hour intervals and sums only valid mm values. Open-Meteo precipitation timestamps END the preceding hour: a 10:20 reference selects a window 11:00 today–11:00 tomorrow and END entries 12:00 today–11:00 tomorrow. Null/missing/non-finite/negative values, unknown clocks, gaps/overlap, duplicates, reversed order, non-hourly intervals and invalid units reject the whole total; no interpolation or calendar-day substitution, including midnight windows. Returns source, retrieval time, interval count and window endpoints; unknown forecast issuance stays null. Home labels “Next 24 full forecast hours: X mm” and both local timestamps. Hour-boundary clock updates do not fetch again.

**Availability/freshness:** Shared fetch now exposes network_failure, timeout, rate_limited, provider_unavailable, provider_http_error and invalid_response with upstream status, Retry-After seconds and validator reason. Public API unavailable status remains HTTP 503; a provider HTTP 503 is never described as malformed scientific data. A received upstream status alone cannot establish publisher-versus-intermediary origin. Browser has at most three transient attempts with 30/60-minute backoff or longer Retry-After; invalid schemas/other rejected requests require manual refresh. Existing abort/location isolation, hidden/offline pause, single request deduplication and six-hour retrieval cadence remain. Current model estimates older than 90 minutes and stale cached receipts are unavailable on Home; manual refresh remains accessible. Optional absence does not falsely imply unsupported variables. Model estimates are not certified station observations.

Proxy allows only exact POST/api/telegram/webhook, rejects other routes/path variants/verbs, ambiguous lengths/transfer encoding/invalid types/header syntax, limits bodies/replies64KiB and concurrency8, and closes slow headers/body/upstream after an overall30-second deadline. Backend still verifies the actual secret. Cloudflare gets a minimal credential-free environment, isolated empty home, explicit /dev/null configuration (preventing user/system ingress discovery), loopback metrics and no raw log echo/persistence. Parse only the owned process pipe for strict HTTPS single-label trycloudflare.com URL. Bounded public DNS and certificate-verified/pinned HTTPS must yield /admin404 and unsigned webhook403 before Telegram setup. Fixed-host TLS Telegram helper has bounded calls, no proxy/redirect/retry, and only getMe/getWebhookInfo/setWebhook; no message sender. Verify bot identity, constrain previous URL replacement through a token-HMAC signed0600 owner receipt, preserve pending updates, allow message updates only/max_connections4, and confirm exact resulting URL/settings. Unrelated registrations stop safely without printing their URL. Receipt is retained across shutdown; no unregister/takeover override. Uncertain API/receipt outcomes are documented, without fabricated ownership/connection.

Separate full-launcher lock remains held through startup/shutdown. Current-boot/user/checkout/start/nonce and pidfd checks constrain cleanup, including detached descendants and crash recovery; unknown proxy/tunnel/listeners are untouched. Normal Ctrl+C130/SIGTERM143 waits for owned children and listening-port release, leaves PostgreSQL running and the stopped temporary webhook registered/offline. Periodic ingress checks/child monitoring fail clearly if Cloudflare fails. No broad kill command or arbitrary port changes.

Verification: combined Python launcher/configuration/process/admin-setup/notification/security/image tests245passed, then final supervisor17passed including two added cases (Ctrl+C during tunnel startup and missing cloudflared). Final collection247distinct tests; all executed successfully across those runs. New-source cases cover complete isolated startup, DNS propagation delay, TLS pinning, ingress rejection, Cloudflare exit/timeout/invalid/changed URL, unrelated listener/webhook, signed owned replacement/tampering/token rotation, missing/invalid0600 configuration, redacted protocol failures, locking, SIGINT/SIGTERM, repeated cycles, detached TERM-resistant workers, SIGKILL recovery, private config/database-fixture preservation and no orphans/credential output. Real local processes/sockets and maintained proxy exercised; Cloudflare, public DNS/HTTPS and Telegram integration outcomes are fake responses in isolated temporary trees with relocated ports/random inert private fixtures. No operational database connection or real Telegram request/registration/send/consent change in tests. Initial sandbox denied local sockets; authorized loopback test reruns passed. One existing fake-launcher regression exposed dependence on occupied live ports; changed only tests/test_launcher.py fixture to allocate temporary ports and assert its actual URL. Existing production ownership/lifecycle code unchanged.

Final review corrected periodic-readiness failure wording so it never implies an earlier registered URL disappeared: it states that no new registration was attempted and any existing registration is preserved. Focused setup/protocol55passed after this wording/test adjustment; no behavior or ownership change.

Frontend278passed/26files; production buildpassed/68modules (existing ~696kB bundle advisory). Existing Starlette/httpx deprecation remains. Bash/Python syntax and diff checks passed. Reviewed source/tests/docs and scanned against current private Telegram values without displaying them; no values found in changed files. .env and data/tmp remain Git-ignored. Actual ./start-full.sh --check and --check --telegram each exited1 safely: backend18050 and frontend14180 already owned by running application processes. Actual ./start.sh --check exited1 because its checkout lock is already held. No running application/proxy/container was stopped or adopted; read-only full check validated private Telegram configuration and located cloudflared before its port refusal. Existing private.env/profile and original launcher source hashes unchanged across checks. Unrelated backend/.env.example and QandA.md preserved/excluded. No live administrator login/password rotation attempted; prior block remains.

Live new-launcher end-to-end status NOT TESTED: the operator must stop the known old launcher's terminal normally, run ./start-full.sh --check --telegram, deliberately run ./start-full.sh --telegram and confirm the verified status. Any unrelated existing webhook requires separately explicit authorized migration; no automatic takeover. Quick Tunnel hostnames are temporary and have no uptime guarantee; no emergency-service/public-deployment claim. ## 2026-10-16 — Home flood/drainage prediction dashboard; page fixes from the per-page review

User request: Home becomes a flood and drainage risk prediction dashboard that shows the riskiest spots around the user's place, from the weather forecast, also drawn on the map. Plus the reviewer's own per-page fixes.

Acceptance: (1) `GET /api/risk/area` scores a fixed 0.05° (~5.5 km) lattice, 7 x 7 cells within Karnataka, around a point from live rain, ground height, mapped historical floods and approved-report counts; missing inputs never score as zero; the response is labelled experimental and `official_warning: false`. (2) Home lists the highest flood/drainage spots (distance, direction, nearest directory place within 3 km), toggles Flood/Drainage, colours each spot on the map with a popup and "Full assessment here", and still fits 1366x768. (3) The statewide grid no longer loses most cells to provider overload.

Changes:
- risk.py: transient provider failures (503/429/timeout/network) are retried twice (2 s, 5 s, honouring Retry-After up to 10 s); grid concurrency 4 → 2; a partly failed grid is cached for 10 minutes instead of 3 hours. Root cause verified live: Open-Meteo answered back-to-back 20-location requests with `503 "The service is overloaded"`; before the fix 236 of 256 grid cells were unavailable, after it 256/256 were scored (live run: 253 low, 3 moderate).
- New area screen (`RiskService.area`, `/api/risk/area`). River flow is deliberately excluded from the area screen (one GloFAS history per cell is too costly) and stays in the point assessment; the UI says so. Report counts use a 2.5 km radius and are labelled as such. Only 6 named localities exist in the active directory, so most spots are named by distance/direction; Overpass was not reachable from this machine and no place names were invented.
- Home: new RiskAreaDashboard (ranked list + counts + calm/partial/unavailable states), coloured map squares with legend, map click → "Check risk here", top card renamed "Risk at your location". A network-estimate location is not ranked (too uncertain).
- Shelters: no longer presents the Bengaluru default as a chosen location; coordinates rounded to 4 decimals; shared map opens framed on Karnataka (also Report).
- Flood Map: live risk status first; historical disclaimer shown only with the historical layer; link points to Home instead of the hidden weather page.
- /weather (hidden): removed "Flood prediction is not available", which contradicted the risk index. The page was not redirected: about 100 test references use it as the weather-session harness; redirecting is deferred.
- Routing: the shelter database is opened only after a routing key exists, so an unconfigured deployment reports `not_configured` instead of a database error (regression test added).
- Tests: CitizenReport focus test waits for the focus effect (was intermittently failing). Shelter layout specs use a labelled empty-live-directory fixture; two specs that need a real PostgreSQL store now skip unless SHELTER_DATABASE_URL is set.

Not done, needs a decision: approved citizen reports were not added as public map pins. The consent reporters accept states "No public publication or model training is authorized", so publishing them requires a new consent version first. Replay (plan 3c) and the validation table (3d) are not built.

Verification: backend 667 passed (1 deselected as documented); frontend unit 283/283 (CitizenReport file 5/5 repeated runs); production build passed; Playwright full suite 84 passed, 8 skipped (6 pre-existing skips + 2 PostgreSQL-only), 0 failed. Live app: area endpoint 22–31 cells scored in ~2 s; Home driven in Chromium at 1366x768 with Kundapur selected, document height 768, no console errors after start-up (one transient /api/weather 503 while the backend was starting).


## 2026-10-16 — Official warnings (IMD, NDMA SACHET) integrated

User request: we cannot predict floods ourselves, so bring in official prediction/warning information and use it in the application.

Research (live, 16 Oct 2026): NDMA SACHET publishes CAP 1.2 alerts per state (Karnataka RSS: https://sachet.ndma.gov.in/cap_public_website/rss/rss_karnataka.xml); messages seen were from IMD Bengaluru via Karnataka SNDMC with severity, expiry and LGD district codes. IMD's documented API (api.imd.gov.in, district-wise warnings) returned HTTP 401 — it needs IP whitelisting via IMD's nodal officer. IMD's public GeoServer layer `imd:district_warnings_india` (used by IMD's own district warning map) is reachable and carries the same fields as the documented API, so the API reference's warning-code and Day colour tables (1 red, 2 orange, 3 yellow, 4 green) are applied; this layer itself is undocumented. CWC's flood forecasting site has no public data API; CWC is a SACHET alert-generating agency. Google Flood Hub's API needs waitlist approval and is not a government source.

Acceptance: official warnings are shown as issued and kept separate from the experimental index; failures read "unavailable — does not mean there is no warning"; expired/cancelled/non-Actual CAP alerts are hidden; old IMD bulletins are not shown as today; admin drafts from official warnings never send without explicit approval.

Changes: `backend/app/official.py` (bounded fetches, DTD/entity-refusing XML parsing, 30 min IMD / 10 min RSS caches, immutable CAP cache, point-in-polygon district lookup, past bulletin days dropped, bulletins ≥2 days old marked stale); `/api/official/warnings`, `/api/official/districts`, admin-only `/api/admin/official/suggestions` (Orange/Red today and tomorrow, source OFFICIAL_WARNING). Home: official one-line status replaces "Official warning feed: Not connected"; "Official warnings" panel (IMD 5-day strip + active SACHET alerts) above the experimental prediction. Flood Map: "Official IMD warnings (today)" layer on by default with counts and legend. Admin: "Suggested alerts from official IMD warnings" fills the composer only. Privacy: access log now strips coordinates from /api/risk, /api/risk/area and /api/official/warnings (previously risk coordinates were logged).

Live result: all 31 Karnataka districts resolved; Udupi Orange today (very heavy rain, thunderstorm), Yellow next three days; statewide 5 Orange · 8 Yellow · 18 Green; 8 admin drafts; SACHET 10 recent messages read, 0 active (3-hour nowcasts expired). Unauthenticated admin suggestions → 401.

Verification: backend 678 passed (1 deselected as documented); frontend unit 288/288; production build passed; Playwright 84 passed, 8 skipped, 0 failed. Live Chromium: Home 1366x768 document height 768, no errors; Flood Map official layer rendered with no errors.

Limitations: SACHET alerts are matched to the user's district by district names in the alert text (LGD codes are carried but the directory's LGD codes are unresolved). The IMD layer is IMD's public map service, not the whitelisted API; for production, request IMD API whitelisting. Official warnings are not added into the experimental score.

## 2026-10-17 — Home current weather disappeared after 90 minutes

User report: current weather not showing in the Home weather box.

Cause: Home shows current conditions only while their valid time is at most 90 minutes old, but the weather session re-fetched only every six hours. In a tab left open, the box read "Current weather unavailable" for roughly 4.5 of every 6 hours. Live check on 17 Oct: `/api/weather` returned current conditions normally (no backend cache), and fresh page loads via GPS, network estimate and map point all showed the temperature, so only elapsed time triggered it.

Acceptance: current conditions are re-fetched 60 minutes after their valid time (never closer than 15 minutes apart), so they are replaced before the 90-minute limit; a provider whose current time stops advancing falls back to the six-hour cycle instead of polling; the six-hour forecast freshness rules are unchanged.

Changes: `useWeatherSession.js` (CURRENT_REFRESH_MS, CURRENT_MIN_GAP_MS). Tests: two new unit tests (hourly refresh; no polling for a stuck reading); three unit tests and `weather-freshness.spec.js` use a reading already older than 60 minutes to keep isolating the six-hour cycle; `location-directory.spec.js` now expects the hourly refresh for the selected point.

Verification: frontend unit 291/291; production build passed; Playwright 82 passed + the 2 adjusted specs re-run passed (12/12 in those files), 8 skipped.

## 2026-10-17 — Report, Shelters, Alerts and Weather page review

Page review verdict: all six pages are needed. Home, Flood Map, Shelters, Report, Alerts and Admin are the product's citizen and operator flows. Weather details is kept but had no route from Home since the seven-day forecast left it.

Acceptance: Report and Shelters maps open on Karnataka rather than India; Report shows a sticky, taller map with a plain instruction for placing the point; Shelters and Alerts show verified official emergency numbers, prominently when no verified shelter is open; Alerts never presents a subscriber link unless configured and points to official warning sources; Home links to the seven-day forecast; "Why?" factor tables render only when opened.

Research (17 Oct 2026): 112 is India's Emergency Response Support System number, live in Karnataka since November 2019 for police, fire, ambulance and disasters (Deccan Herald / The News Minute launch reports, factly.in ERSS explainer). 080-1070 (toll-free) is the Karnataka State Emergency Operation Centre, Revenue Department (Disaster Management), listed in 2021 and 2023 government statements. Undated flood-season WhatsApp/control-room numbers were not used.

Changes: `OfficialHelp.jsx` (112, 080-1070, tel: links); `ShelterMap.jsx` 0.25 zoom steps and a post-layout Karnataka refit when no points or district bounds apply (affects Report, Shelters and Admin maps); `CitizenReport.jsx` map hint and side-by-side location buttons, sticky map; `AlertSubscription.jsx` three-step "how alerts work" and "Official warnings you can use now" (Home, NDMA SACHET, IMD district warnings); Home weather line "7-day forecast" link; `RiskAreaDashboard.jsx` renders factor tables only after "Why?" opens — this fixes the intermittent `home-location-river.spec` table-count failure noted in the previous entry (tables appeared inside the closed section when risk data arrived).

## 2026-10-17 — In-app navigation to shelters with Google Maps

User request: navigate from the user's location to a shelter through Google Maps inside the application.

Research (Google documentation, 17 Oct 2026): the Maps Embed API directions mode (`https://www.google.com/maps/embed/v1/directions`) is the official way to show a Google route inside a page; it accepts latitude/longitude origin and destination and modes walking/driving/bicycling/transit; it "is free to use with unlimited requests" but "an API key is required for all Maps Embed API requests". Live turn-by-turn navigation cannot run inside a web page: Maps URLs (`https://www.google.com/maps/dir/?api=1`) with `dir_action=navigate` launch turn-by-turn in the Google Maps app (Android/iOS) or open the browser, and need no key. The unofficial keyless embed (`output=embed`) was not used.

Acceptance: after "Get directions" revalidates the shelter, an in-app panel replaces the shelter map with the Google route (Walking/Driving) from the user's location; "Start navigation in Google Maps" (dir_action=navigate) is available only for five seconds after a recheck, as before; "Recheck and start navigation" repeats the revalidation; if the shelter drops out of the refreshed directory the panel stops navigation; without a key the panel shows the start point and shelter on the app map and the launch button still works; the panel states that Google's route is not checked for flooding and that coordinates are sent to Google; demonstration shelters stay labelled not for travel.

Changes: backend `GET /api/navigation/embed` (reads optional `GOOGLE_MAPS_EMBED_API_KEY`; reports it only when well formed; `Cache-Control: no-store`); `ShelterNavigation.jsx` (official Embed and Maps URLs built from coordinates only; iframe with `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` so a referrer-restricted key works; fallback map); `PublicShelters.jsx` opens the panel after a successful recheck; CSS; README setup note. No key is committed; `.env.example` left untouched.

Verification: backend 682 passed (4 new embed-configuration tests), 1 deselected as documented; frontend unit 299/299 (3 new navigation tests; one directory-revalidation test now counts only `/api/shelters` calls because the panel makes a one-time configuration request); build passed; Playwright 84 passed, 8 skipped. Live Chromium with labelled fixtures: no-key path shows the fallback map and a working launch link (travelmode=walking, dir_action=navigate), which reverts to "Recheck and start navigation" after five seconds; keyed path verified with a fake key-shaped fixture and a local placeholder intercepting the Google embed request, so no request reached Google. Restarted app: `/api/navigation/embed` → not_configured.

Limitations: a real Google route preview needs your Google Cloud key (Maps Embed API enabled, HTTP-referrer restricted). Google routes are generic and not flood-aware. An intermittent `HistoricalFloodMap.test.jsx` deep-equality failure occurred once in a full run and passed 3/3 in isolation and in the next full run.

## 2026-10-17 — Administrator panel made easier to use

User request: understand the Admin panel, list how to make it user friendly, and implement it.

Findings (walkthrough with labelled admin fixtures served in the browser; no real credentials used): no overview of what needs attention; an Open shelter whose verification had expired showed only "Unavailable to public" with no reason (the real shelter would silently disappear at expiry); every row had two full-width buttons repeating the facility name; "Edit" filled a form ~1,500 px below with no scroll; no quick re-verify; no feedback on whether an entrance pin was set; notification composer below the suggestion lists and "Use as draft" filled an off-screen box; report queue without counts, filter or description, with the selected report's evidence below the queue and the map pin not focused; duplicated page titles.

Acceptance: all existing controls unchanged (session, CSRF, verification checklist and evidence, material-change reset, 24 h expiry, Pending-first creation, explicit preview/approve/send-once, audit). Shelter workspace shows counts (visible / need attention / full / closed), a "Show" filter, compact cards with status label, a plain reason for public visibility (expires in N h; expired N h ago — re-verify; pending; full; closed), occupancy bar, and Edit / History / Re-verify buttons; Edit scrolls to the form and focuses its heading, Re-verify scrolls to the verification checklist, "Stop editing" exits; the entrance pin status is shown; a badge beside the tabs opens the shelters needing attention. Notifications: compose and send on the left, suggested drafts on the right, "Use as draft" focuses the message and states the draft's source, compact history with status labels. Reports: counts, a "Show" filter, waiting reports listed first, description snippets with age and accuracy, evidence panel beside the map with the selected pin focused, pagination only when needed.

Changes: `AdminShelters.jsx` (`shelterState` derives explanations only from server fields; falls back to verified_at + 24 h if a row lacks the expiry), `AdminNotifications.jsx`, `AdminReports.jsx`, `App.jsx` (admin page intro visually hidden), CSS. Accessible names used by tests and assistive technology are kept ("Edit <name>", "Audit <name>", "waterlogging · SUBMITTED", tab names).

Also fixed: `HistoricalFloodMap.test.jsx` race (waited for source text that can render before the geometry redraw) — now waits for the redraw; 3 consecutive full unit runs passed.

Verification: frontend unit 300/300 ×3 (new `AdminShelterState.test.jsx`); build passed; Playwright 84 passed, 8 skipped (PostgreSQL-only, including end-to-end admin flows — not run here). Live Chromium with fixtures: counts 1 visible / 2 need attention / 1 full / 0 closed; reasons rendered for all four states; Re-verify scrolls to the checklist; "Use as draft" fills and focuses the message; selecting a report opens its evidence beside the map; no page errors.

## 2026-10-17 — Admin: map click fills facility name, address and district

User request: when the administrator selects a location on the admin map, automatically fetch the place name, address and district.

Research (17 Oct 2026): BigDataCloud's free client-side reverse geocoding (used on Home for the device's own location) prohibits converting coordinates other than the current device location and server relaying, so it is not used here. Nominatim usage policy (operations.osmfoundation.org/policies/nominatim): at most 1 request/s per application, identifying User-Agent or Referer, results must be cached, visible OSM attribution, no auto-complete or systematic/grid queries; data ODbL 1.0. Live check: Nominatim reachable; `layer=poi` returns the nearest named place (e.g. Sri Anantheshwara Temple, Udupi) where the default reverse lookup returns the street.

Decision reconciled explicitly: the existing "no public Nominatim fallback" rule protects citizen GPS on Home and is unchanged. This exception covers only an authenticated administrator's explicit click on a facility entrance (not personal location), sent from the backend, rate-limited, cached and attributed.

Acceptance: `/api/admin/place` requires an administrator session (401 otherwise); district by point-in-polygon on IMD's 31 Karnataka district boundaries mapped to the directory through the existing alias table (OSM `state_district` only as a fallback, labelled); a facility name is auto-filled only when the nearest named place is within 75 m of the click, offered with its distance (button only) when 75–300 m away, otherwise not suggested; address composed from OSM address parts; points outside Karnataka make no external request; outages say "type manually"; the form fills the district, and the name/address only when empty — typed text is never overwritten ("Use this name"/"Use this address" buttons); filled fields go through the existing material-change rule, so verification confirmations are cleared; attribution "© OpenStreetMap contributors, ODbL 1.0, via Nominatim" shown; coordinates removed from the access log.

Changes: `backend/app/admin_place.py` (PlaceLookup: application-wide lock with ≥1.1 s spacing, 24 h LRU cache of 512 points, bounded 256 KiB responses, User-Agent "FloodPulse/2 (college project; administrator shelter entrance lookup)" — no personal contact data); `official.read_bounded` accepts headers; `main.py` wiring; `home_sources.py` log stripping; `AdminShelters.jsx` lookup panel; map-picked coordinates rounded to 6 decimals; CSS.

Bugs caught by the new tests before release: city/town/municipality selection took the first (possibly empty) field and dropped "Manipal"; the frontend used Python's `casefold()` (not JavaScript) so every lookup failed.

Verification: backend 692 passed (9 new in `test_admin_place.py`: login required, fill + attribution + User-Agent, roads not offered as names, cache and 1 req/s, outside Karnataka makes no request, provider outage, IMD outage fallback, far place only offered, address composition), 1 deselected as documented; frontend unit 301/301 (new map-click test: fills empty name, keeps typed address, sets district, offers "Use this address"); build passed; Playwright 84 passed, 8 skipped. Live (real IMD + Nominatim, login check bypassed in a local harness, 3 requests in total): Malpe–Manipal Road point → Udupi, address only, no name; temple point → "Sri Anantheshwara Temple" 23 m, "Rathabidi, Brahmagiri, Udupi, Karnataka 576101", Udupi; Chennai point → outside, no request. Restarted app: unauthenticated `/api/admin/place` → 401.

Limitations: OpenStreetMap names and addresses can be missing or outdated — suggestions only, confirmed on site; IMD district polygons come from IMD's public map service, not a certified administrative boundary.

## 2026-10-17 — OpenRouteService live; flood-aware route in the navigation panel; auto-locate; "Go to nearest open shelter"

User request: implement OpenRouteService routing, (1) ask for location automatically when directions are requested, and (4) a Home action to reach the nearest open shelter.

Research: openrouteservice `options.avoid_polygons` accepts GeoJSON Polygon/MultiPolygon (official routing-options docs); the official plans page (now redirecting to account.heigit.org) lists the free Standard plan Directions quota at 2,000/day and 40/min, non-commercial. The 13 Oct decision forbids restoring Home shelter sections but permits "a compact direct shelter link or safety action".

Live evidence (key loaded from the private .env; value never printed): `/api/route/status` configured; Udupi centre → the verified shelter: walking 6.92 km / 83 min / 39 steps, driving 6.9 km / 9 min / 15 steps, 0 approved reports on the corridor, label "NOT certified flood-safe". Avoidance proven with one direct test request carrying a 150 m avoid octagon on the route midpoint: 6.92 → 7.26 km, closest approach 229 m. No fabricated report was created.

Acceptance: the navigation panel offers "Avoids reported flooding" (default; OpenRouteService route drawn on the map with distance, time, avoided-report count, safety label, turn-by-turn steps, attribution) and "Google route"; the option disappears when routing is not configured; malformed route replies show "unavailable", never a broken route; "Start navigation in Google Maps" sits above the route so it is always visible; "Get directions" without a usable start asks for location, then rechecks availability and opens directions automatically; refusal or an imprecise fix (> 100 m or stale) never yields directions and explains why; Home's "Go to nearest open shelter" (no shelter request on Home) opens Shelters, locates if needed, picks the nearest open shelter and opens its directions; each press is handled once; distances shown to one decimal.

Fixed during verification: the route-source switch was squashed to zero height in the fixed-height desktop panel (flex shrink); a refused location dropped the original "adequate starting location" explanation (caught by shelter-integrity.spec) — both now shown.

Verification: frontend unit 306/306 (new: flood-aware route first, option hidden when unconfigured, auto-locate then directions, Home nearest request, imprecise location refused); build passed; Playwright full run 83 passed + the corrected shelter-integrity spec re-run with shelters/home specs 23 passed, 2 skipped (8 skipped in the full run are PostgreSQL-only). Live Chromium desktop and 390 px phone: Home → Use my location → Go to nearest open shelter → Shelters directions panel with the OpenRouteService route (6.9 km, ~83 min walking); no page errors.

## 2026-10-17 — Shelters: "Get directions" works on laptops (choose start on the map)

User report: Get directions on the Shelters page is not working.

Reproduced live (app unchanged since 4bf738b): precise GPS (±25 m) opened the directions panel with the OpenRouteService route; location refused → "Location unavailable or permission denied"; Wi-Fi-level accuracy (±800 m, typical for laptops) → "too uncertain". Both refusals come from the existing navigation-origin rule (GPS ≤ 100 m and ≤ 5 min, or an explicitly chosen point), which is kept. The page's only fallback was the collapsed "Change location" control, which on Shelters has no map (5 searchable localities or typed coordinates), so laptops had no practical way to get directions.

Acceptance: when location is refused or too imprecise, a "Choose my start on the map" button appears; it enables a one-time start-point click on the shelter map (map clicks do nothing otherwise), the chosen point is labelled "Start point chosen on the map", and directions continue automatically with the usual availability recheck. This uses the already documented option "Choose a starting point on the map", so no safety rule is relaxed.

Verification: frontend unit 307/307 (new test: refusal → choose on map → directions; map inert until requested); build passed; Playwright 84 passed, 8 skipped. Live Chromium: desktop with ±800 m location → fallback → 2.1 km / 25 min walking route; phone with location refused → fallback → 0.7 km / 9 min route; no page errors.

Data issue observed (not changed): the live public shelter shows test text in Restrictions ("awrg") and Approved contact; the administrator should correct it.

## 2026-10-17 — Opt-in host allowance for a temporary phone test

User request: test the application on a mobile phone.

Findings: phone browsers expose geolocation only in a secure context (HTTPS or localhost), so plain Wi-Fi access (http://192.168…) cannot test "Use my location" or directions. A Cloudflare quick tunnel (cloudflared already installed) gives a temporary HTTPS address. Vite 7 rejects unknown Host headers by default, and citizen report submission and administrator login require the configured SHELTER_PUBLIC_ORIGIN.

Change: `frontend/vite.config.js` reads optional `FLOODPULSE_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (comma-separated, e.g. `.trycloudflare.com`); empty by default, so behaviour is unchanged unless explicitly enabled. The launcher already passes non-secret variables to Vite. No tunnel was started.

Verification: frontend unit 307/307; build passed; config reviewed.

## 2026-10-18 — Report: in-app GPS camera

User request: integrate a GPS camera on the Report page so a flood photo records its location, address and time, building a flood report record for future AI modelling.

Design decisions: live in-app camera (getUserMedia, rear camera) so the photo is captured now, not picked from the gallery; continuous high-accuracy GPS (watchPosition) and the shutter disabled until a fix exists; the uploaded image stays clean — no text burned into pixels (stamps would contaminate any later image analysis); location, accuracy and time are structured report data; a stamped copy can be saved locally only. The place name uses BigDataCloud's free client API, which its fair-use policy permits for the device's own current location; it is shown to the person and not sent to or stored by FloodPulse. The district is computed on the server from IMD's cached district boundaries — no citizen coordinates go to a third party.

No database migration (demonstration on 19 Oct): the optional `capture` block (`source: IN_APP_GPS_CAMERA`, `captured_at`) is validated strictly (photo and browser GPS fix required; extra fields rejected) and stored inside the existing `image_metadata` JSONB as `capture` (`client_reported: true`, capture time, GPS fix time, gap in seconds, accuracy, flags `CAPTURE_GPS_GAP_OVER_60S` / `CAPTURE_TIME_OUT_OF_RANGE`) plus `district`. The client sends `capture` only while the report's location is still that photo's own GPS fix. Moderators see "In-app GPS camera photo (client-reported) … GPS ±12 m, fix 4 s from the photo" and the district.

Unchanged: consent text ("No public publication or model training is authorized"), 7-day photo / 30-day report retention, EXIF consent and sanitisation, `training_eligible: false` in the evidence export, USER_REPORTED until moderation. Using reports for future AI modelling needs a separate decision (opt-in research consent version and retention) — not implemented.

Verification: backend 695 passed (new `test_report_capture.py`: capture requires photo + live GPS, extra fields rejected, record labelled client-reported with gap flags, district from IMD boundaries without external lookup), 1 deselected; frontend unit 313 (new `GpsCamera.test.jsx` and report/admin tests); build passed; Playwright 83 passed + updated ui-ux keyboard-order spec and reports spec 5 passed, 1 skipped. Live Chromium (fake camera, emulated GPS ±12 m, place lookup answered by a fixture, submission intercepted so nothing was stored): camera overlay showed place, coordinates and time; capture → preview with Use/Retake/Save stamped copy; payload contained the photo, BROWSER_GPS ±12 m, gps_at 8 s before captured_at and capture.source IN_APP_GPS_CAMERA; no page errors. Real-phone camera not yet tested (needs the HTTPS phone link).

User request: analyse the Report page, list improvements and implement them.

Findings (walkthrough of every state on desktop and a 390 px phone, submissions intercepted): no severity information (water depth, road usability); observation time defaulted to page-load time with no shortcuts; on phones the manual-location map sat below the whole form and the uncertainty radius had to be typed in metres; GPS showed only coordinates; no preview for gallery photos and three equal-weight photo buttons; readiness was a long sentence; review used raw values ("waterlogging", US dates) without the photo; receipt was technical with no "report another"; no emergency number; map texts used internal codes ("USER_REPORTED GPS", "EXIF", "vision model").

Unchanged: consent text, retention, USER_REPORTED until moderation, explicit review and submit, withdrawal, same-origin submission.

Verification: frontend unit 312/312 (new test: time shortcut, depth/road line in the submitted description, place label, checklist, receipt reset); build passed; Playwright 83 passed + updated ui-ux keyboard order (emergency links → GPS camera → Event type) with reports and mvp-e2e specs 5 passed, 2 skipped. Live Chromium desktop (GPS) and phone (manual map inside step 2, "Same street ±50 m"), place lookup answered by a fixture and submissions intercepted so nothing was stored; no page errors.

Follow-up after the demonstration: store depth and road usability as proper columns (additive migration) instead of a description prefix.

## 2026-10-18 — Production hosting package (Azure for Students, any Linux server)

User request: host the project (required for the major project); Azure for Students chosen (college email, no card). Demonstration on 22 October.

Research (18 Oct 2026): Render free services sleep after 15 minutes idle and free Postgres expires after 30 days; the DigitalOcean student credit reportedly ended 31 July 2026; Oracle Always Free needs a Visa/Mastercard. Azure for Students gives US$100 credit for 12 months without a card plus 750 hours/month of burstable VMs (B1s, B2pts v2, B2ats v2; official pages differ on whether always free or 12 months). Azure public IPs support a free DNS name label (<label>.<region>.cloudapp.azure.com).

Design: one VM with Docker Compose — `postgis/postgis:16-3.4` (no published port), the FastAPI app serving the built site from the same origin, and `caddy:2.8.4` for automatic Let's Encrypt HTTPS. The launcher's loopback-only rules are unchanged; production uses its own configuration. Existing security controls preserved: two restricted logins (read-only directory reader; app role granted exactly `mvp_database.APP_PRIVILEGES`, verified by its own least-privilege check), the owner login used only by one-off setup/admin commands, Secure cookies with an exact HTTPS origin, frame-denial/nosniff/referrer headers on static files, admin passwords typed privately and scrypt-hashed, no secrets in the image or repository.

Changes: `backend/app/static_site.py` (enabled only by `FLOODPULSE_STATIC_DIR`; `/api/*` never falls back to the page; path traversal refused; fingerprinted assets cached, index not cached; `.webmanifest` type) and its wiring in `main.py`; `Dockerfile` (Node 22 build stage, Python 3.12 slim runtime, non-root user, single worker, only runtime data copied); `.dockerignore` (secrets, local data, node_modules, venvs excluded); `deploy/docker-compose.yml`, `Caddyfile`, `db-init/20-floodpulse-roles.sh`, `setup.sh` (generates passwords into private `deploy/.env`, migrates, imports places, grants, starts, verifies; safe to rerun for updates), `create-admin.sh`, `telegram_webhook.py` (sets the bot webhook with the secret token; never prints secrets), `env.example`; `docs/AZURE_HOSTING.md`; README pointer.

Verification: backend 697 passed (2 new static-site tests), 1 deselected; shell scripts syntax-checked. Full local rehearsal with test settings (domain localhost, ports 8080/8443, separate project name, no real keys): `setup.sh` built and started everything and its least-privilege check passed; over HTTPS `/`, `/shelters`, API routes and the manifest returned 200, `/api/nope` 404 JSON, HSTS/X-Frame-Options/nosniff present, HTTP redirected to HTTPS; in Chromium through Caddy, live weather, IMD card and risk loaded, a labelled test report was stored, a test-only administrator signed in with Secure cookies, saved a Pending test shelter, saw the report in moderation and signed out, no page errors; a second `setup.sh` run reported the place directory already imported and kept the data. The test stack, its volumes, the test `.env` and the test admin password file were removed afterwards; the laptop app was unaffected. Not yet done on Azure itself (needs the user's account).

User report: the Azure for Students offer is not available for their college email; asked whether the same VM can be used on AWS.

Research (18 Oct 2026): AWS accounts created after 15 July 2025 get a Free plan with US$100 credit (+ up to US$100 for onboarding tasks) for 6 months; the old 12-month free t2/t3.micro allowance applies only to older accounts. AWS India accepts Visa, Mastercard, RuPay cards and UPI; sign-up verification charges and refunds ₹2. The deployment package itself is cloud-neutral and unchanged.

## 2026-10-19 — 26-point production-readiness audit and fixes

User request: apply a widely shared 26-point production checklist (rate limiting, validation, secrets, dependencies, error leakage, uploads, monitoring, backups, concurrency, performance) where applicable; explain any item that cannot or need not be done.

Audit results (evidence in brackets):
| # | Check | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 1, 21 | Rate limiting | Partly present: admin login 5/account and 20/address per 15 min, reports 5/address and 60 total per 15 min, both PostgreSQL-backed. **Added** per-address limits for public API reads (300/min) and expensive endpoints (area risk, routing, AI outlook, admin place: 30/min). Exponential backoff not added: the existing fixed 15-minute windows are kept to avoid changing tested authentication behaviour. |
| 2, 3 | API limits / spending caps | **Added** an OpenRouteService daily cap (1,500 of the free 2,000). Google Maps Embed is free and unlimited; Open-Meteo and IMD/SACHET responses are cached. Cloud spending caps are an account setting (AWS/Azure budget alerts, in the hosting guides). |
| 4, 7, 8, 25 | Errors, failed requests, timeouts, leakage | Present: bounded provider timeouts (10–20 s), frontend aborts at 10–15 s, "unavailable" states everywhere, validation errors on admin/report routes replaced by generic text. **Added** a catch-all handler: visitors get a generic message and an error ID; method, path (no query string, since it can hold coordinates) and the traceback go to the server log. IMD parser errors now say "invalid response". |
| 5, 6 | Loading / empty states | Present on every page (verified in the 18 Oct audit). |
| 9 | Duplicate submissions | Present: reports and Telegram notices use request IDs (same content returns the original receipt; changed content is refused); shelters use revision checks; buttons disable while busy. |
| 10 | Duplicate payments | Not applicable: no payments. |
| 11, 12, 13 | Queries, indexes, pagination | Present: indexes on shelter status/verification, entrance (GiST), audit history, session expiry, notification history, report position (GiST) and received time; admin lists paginate at 50–100. |
| 14, 15, 26 | Uploads | Present: photos decoded with Pillow (not trusted by extension), MIME must match content, 5 MiB and 10 megapixel limits, decompression-bomb guard, re-encoded to strip metadata, stored in PostgreSQL behind admin authentication, never executable; 7 MiB request limit; Caddy 8 MB limit; the GPS camera compresses before upload. |
| 16 | Caching | Present and safe: provider data cached with expiry; admin, shelter and report responses are no-store. |
| 17 | Uptime monitoring | **Added** `/api/health/ready` (checks the database without revealing details), Docker health check for the app (Caddy waits for it), UptimeRobot instructions. |
| 18 | Error logging | **Added** server-side error log with IDs; Docker logs capped at 3 × 10 MB per service. |
| 19 | Simultaneous users | **Tested**: 50 and 100 simultaneous visitors (400 and 800 Home requests) through the HTTPS stack: 0 failures; median 0.8–1.0 s, 95th percentile 4.4–4.8 s (first calls to weather providers); total memory ~270 MB. Script `scripts/load_test.py`. |
| 20 | Backup restoration | **Added** `deploy/backup.sh` (custom-format dump, private folder, newest 14 kept) and `deploy/restore.sh` (typed confirmation, stops the app, restores in one transaction, re-checks permissions). **Tested**: backup → added a record → restore → record gone, permissions check passed, readiness ok. |
| 22 | Input validation | Present: every request body uses strict Pydantic models with unknown fields forbidden; numeric ranges on coordinates; the three free-text path parameters are length/allow-list checked. |
| 23 | Secrets | Clean: tracked files contain only placeholders and variable references; the full git history (all branches, 168 commits) has no Google, Telegram, OpenRouteService, AWS, GitHub or private-key patterns and no `.env` file was ever committed; the built frontend contains only a key-format check, not a key. The Google Embed key is public by design (restricted by website). |
| 24 | Dependency audit | Clean: `npm audit` 0 vulnerabilities (all and production); `pip-audit` on backend requirements: no known vulnerabilities. |

Security decision reconciled explicitly: the app previously ignored forwarded client addresses. In the hosted stack it now trusts `X-Forwarded-For` only from Caddy's fixed internal address (172.28.0.10); Caddy replaces any client-sent value. Verified: a request through Caddy with a forged `X-Forwarded-For: 203.0.113.99` was logged with the real connection address. Without this, every visitor shared one login and report limit. The laptop launcher is unchanged.

Changes: `backend/app/rate_limit.py`, `main.py` (limiter, error handler, logger, `/api/health/ready`), `routing.py` (daily cap), `shelter_security.env_limit` with configurable login/report thresholds, `official.py` (generic parser error); frontend shows the server's "please wait" message (`useRisk.js`, `FloodIntelligenceMap.jsx`); Playwright test server gets high limits; `deploy/docker-compose.yml` (trusted proxy, fixed network, app health check, log caps, limit settings), `deploy/backup.sh`, `deploy/restore.sh`, `.gitignore`/`.dockerignore` for backups; `scripts/load_test.py`; hosting guides (backups with cron, UptimeRobot, limits table).

Verification: backend 706 passed (new: error handling 3, rate limit 6, static site 2 earlier); frontend unit 312/312; build passed; Playwright 84 passed, 8 skipped; local HTTPS stack rehearsal as described (readiness, forged-address test, 31st expensive request → 429 with Retry-After 60, load tests, backup/restore). Test stack, test `.env` and test backups removed.

Remaining risks: a single worker with in-process limits (fine for one server; multiple servers would need a shared store); per-account login lockout can be triggered by someone guessing an admin username (15-minute window); no malware scanning of photos (they are decoded and re-encoded, never executed or published); provider free-tier limits (Open-Meteo non-commercial terms) apply.

## 2026-10-19 — Production: IMD and SACHET unreachable from the AWS server (diagnosis)

User report: on the hosted site (floodpulse-smvitm.duckdns.org) the official IMD and NDMA SACHET cards say "could not be retrieved (network failure)", while weather and risk work; on the laptop all work.

Findings: the server is 16.176.0.72, AWS Sydney (ap-southeast-2, AS16509). Both government endpoints answered 200 from check-host.net probes in Canada, the UK, the US, Israel, Russia, Serbia, India and Australia, so this is not a country block. The same Docker image run on the laptop (India) fetched SACHET (200, 8 KB) and IMD (200, 717 KB), so the image and code are not at fault. Open-Meteo works from the server, so its network is up. Remaining explanations: the government sites block AWS address ranges (common for .gov.in web firewalls), or an IPv6/route issue specific to the instance. Not yet proven: needs a test on the server.

Changes: `official.read_bounded` logs the exact failure (host, exception type, message) server-side while visitors still see "network failure"; `deploy/check-sources.sh` tests IMD, SACHET and Open-Meteo from the server (IPv4 and IPv6) and from inside the app container, and prints recent official-source errors.

Verification: official and error-handling tests 14 passed; script syntax-checked.

## 2026-10-19 — Server check result; setup.sh upgrades the old network automatically

Server output (user screenshot): from the AWS host over IPv4, IMD 200 (2.0 s), SACHET 200 (1.1 s), Open-Meteo 200 — so the government sites do not block the server; IPv6 fails because those hosts have no IPv6 address (curl exit 6, expected). The update itself had failed: the new fixed internal network (172.28.0.0/24) could not replace the existing network while the old containers were attached ("network has active endpoints"), so the old app version kept running and the in-container test could not start.

Change: `deploy/setup.sh` detects an existing project network with a different subnet and runs `docker compose down` first (volumes, therefore data, are kept).

Verification (local, test settings): deployed the previous compose file (commit 6dbb9e2) with a marker table, then ran the new setup.sh: it reported the network update, recreated the network as 172.28.0.0/24, all services healthy, least-privilege check passed, marker data still present (42). `check-sources.sh` on that stack: host IPv4/IPv6 and in-container IMD, SACHET, Open-Meteo all 200; hosted `/api/official/warnings` returned IMD and SACHET available. Test stack and settings removed.

## 2026-10-19 — Fix: setup.sh exited silently when an optional setting was absent

User screenshot: after `git pull && ./setup.sh`, the script stopped right after building the image (no network update, old containers still running; check-sources again hit "network has active endpoints").

Cause: `value()` in setup.sh pipes `grep` under `set -euo pipefail`; for a key missing from deploy/.env (here `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME`, used by the new network check), grep's "no match" status failed the command substitution and the script exited without a message. The earlier local upgrade test used a test .env that contained `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME`, so it did not reveal this. `create-admin.sh` had the same pattern for a missing `MAINTENANCE_PASSWORD` (it would exit before printing "Run ./setup.sh first").

Fix: both lookups tolerate a missing key (`{ grep … || true; }`).

Verification without touching real Docker data (the laptop's real database volume is floodpulse_sprint6_data, so a local project named "floodpulse" was not used): ran setup.sh with a fake `docker` that records calls and reports an old 172.21.0.0/16 network, and an .env like the user's (domain only). Old version: exit 1 after `docker compose build app`, 1 step printed — reproduces the screenshot. Fixed version: exit 0, all steps printed, calls in order build → network inspect → compose down → up db → migrations → import → grants → up app caddy → permission check; passwords and FLOODPULSE_ORIGIN appended to .env.
